\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C})_{T^{\prime}}=\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C}_{T^{\prime}})
. Thus
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}
is
well-defined.

By virtue of (
6.2
), the functors
T\mapsto\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
and
T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
are
representable by the
S
-schemes
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
and
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. Thus the map of functors
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}
is
representable by a map of
S
-schemes
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}
.

Owing to (
5.6
)(1), the ideal of
\mathscr{C}
is equal to
\mathop{\mathscr{H}\kern-1.0pt\textit{om\/}}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}(\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C}),\,\mathscr{B}_{T})
. Thus
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}\circ\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}
is the identity. Similarly, so is

\lambda^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}\circ\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}
. Thus
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}
is an
isomorphism, and
(\lambda^{\mathbf{h}})^{-1}=\lambda^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
.

Plainly,
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}})\subset\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
. Similarly,
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}(\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}})\subset\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. But
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}

and
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
are inverse isomorphisms. Thus
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}})=\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
. Thus (1)
holds.

For (2),
form the Hilbert-flag scheme
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}
. Its
T
-points are the pairs
(\mathscr{B}^{\diamond},\,\mathscr{C}^{\diamond})

where
\mathscr{B}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}^{\mathbf{b}}
and
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\diamond}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. Thus
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}=\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}}^{\mathbf{h}}
where
\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}
is the
universal quotient on
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b}}
; moreover,
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b}}
and
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}}^{\mathbf{h}}
exist by
virtue of (
6.2
). Form the two forgetful maps
\varphi^{\mathbf{b}}\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b}}
and

\varphi^{\mathbf{h}}\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. They represent the maps of functors given by
\varphi^{\mathbf{b}}(\mathscr{B}^{\diamond},\,\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}):=\mathscr{B}^{\diamond}
and
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{B}^{\diamond},\,\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}):=\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}
.

Let’s prove that
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}}
is smooth at
(\mathscr{B}_{R},\,\mathscr{C})
. To do
so, let’s use the Infinitesimal Criterion
[
EGAIV
]
*(17.14.1),
p. 98: Given a local
S
-scheme
Y
, a closed subscheme
X
of
Y
, an

S
-map
\psi\colon Y\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
that carries the closed
point of
Y
to
\mathscr{C}
, and an
S
-map
\xi\colon X\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}
that carries the closed point of
X
to
(\mathscr{B}_{R},\,\mathscr{C})
and that lifts
\psi|X
, we must lift
\psi
to an
S
-map

\chi\colon Y\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}
that restricts to

\xi
.

Let
\mathscr{I}
be the ideal of
\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}
in the pullback of
\mathscr{A}
to

\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b}}
. Then
\mathscr{I}_{R}
is the ideal of
\mathscr{B}_{R}

in
\mathscr{A}_{R}
. So by hypothesis,
\mathscr{I}_{R}
is generated by a homogeneous
regular sequence, say
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{r}
. But
\mathscr{B}_{R}
is Artinian. So
the
f_{i}
may be replaced by any
r
homogeneous generators. But say

K_{0}
is the residue field of the scheme point of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b}}
that represents
\mathscr{B}_{R}
, and set
R_{0}:=\mathop{\rm Spec}(K_{0})
.
Then
\mathscr{I}_{R}=\mathscr{I}_{R_{0}}\otimes_{K_{0}}K
. So take the
f_{i}
to form a
minimal set of homogeneous generators of
\mathscr{I}_{R_{0}}
. Plainly the

f_{i}
generate
\mathscr{I}_{R}
. If they no longer form a minimal set, then
some
f_{i}
is a
K
-linear combination of monomials in the other
f_{i}
.
But
K
and
K_{0}
are fields. So
f_{i}
is a
K_{0}
-linear combination of
the same monomials, contrary to the minimality of the
f_{i}
. Thus the

f_{i}
form a regular sequence of homogeneous generators of
\mathscr{I}_{R_{0}}
.

Let
K_{1}
be the residue field of
X
, and set
R_{1}:=\mathop{\rm Spec}(K_{1})
. The
composition
\varphi^{\mathbf{b}}\circ\xi\colon X\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b}}

induces an injection
K_{0}\hookrightarrow K_{1}
. Via it, the
f_{i}
form a regular
sequence of homogeneous generators of
\mathscr{I}_{R_{1}}
, which is the ideal
of
\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}_{R_{1}}
in
\mathscr{A}_{R_{1}}
. Since
X
is a local scheme,
each
f_{i}
is the residue of some homogeneous
F_{i}\in\Gamma(\mathscr{I}_{X})
.
By Nakayama’s lemma, the
F_{i}
generate
\mathscr{I}_{X}
. By the local
criterion of flatness, the
F_{i}
form a regular sequence.

Let
\mathscr{J}
be the ideal of the universal quotient on
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
of the pullback of
\mathscr{A}
. The map
\xi\colon X\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}
yields an inclusion
\mathscr{I}_{X}\subset\mathscr{J}_{X}
. But
X
is a closed subscheme of
Y
. So
\mathscr{J}_{X}
is a quotient
of
\mathscr{J}_{Y}
, where
\mathscr{J}_{Y}
is the pullback of
\mathscr{J}
via
\psi\colon Y\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. Since
\mathscr{J}_{Y}
is homogeneous, each

F_{i}
is the residue of some homogeneous
\widetilde{F}_{i}\in\Gamma(\mathscr{J}_{Y})
.
Let
\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
be the ideal the
\widetilde{F}_{i}
generate in
\mathscr{J}_{Y}
, and
set
\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}:=\mathscr{A}_{Y}/\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
. Then
\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{X}=\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}_{X}
. By the local criterion of flatness,
\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}
is
Y
-flat.
Hence
\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}_{Y}}^{\mathbf{b}}
. Set
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}=\mathscr{A}_{Y}/\mathscr{J}_{Y}
. Then
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. Thus the pair
(\widetilde{\mathscr{B}},\,\widetilde{\mathscr{C}})
defines a map

Y\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}
that restricts to
\xi
, as
desired. Thus
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}}\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is smooth at
(\mathscr{B}_{R},\,\mathscr{C})
.

Finally, suppose
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is
S
-smooth at
\mathscr{C}
.
Now,
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}}\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is smooth at
(\mathscr{B}_{R},\,\mathscr{C})
. Hence
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}
is
S
-smooth at
(\mathscr{B}_{R},\,\mathscr{C})
.
But
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}=\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, as noted early in this proof; similarly,
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}=\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
. So let
U
be the largest
open subscheme of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b}}
on which
\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}
is

U
-Gorenstein. Then
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}_{U}}^{\mathbf{h}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}_{U}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
owing to (1) with
U
for

S
. By hypothesis,
\mathscr{B}_{R}
is an
R
-point of
U
. Thus
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
is smooth at
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{B}_{R},\,\mathscr{C})
, which is
(\mathscr{B}_{R},\,\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C}))
. But
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}

is smooth at
(\mathscr{B}_{R},\,,\lambda^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}(\mathscr{C}))
, just as
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}}\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}

is smooth at
(\mathscr{B}_{R},\,\mathscr{C})
. Thus
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
is

S
-smooth at
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C})
, as desired. Similarly, if

\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
is
S
-smooth at
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C})
,
then
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is
S
-smooth at
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}(\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C}))
. But
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}=(\lambda^{\mathbf{h}})^{-1}
by (1). Thus (2) holds.
∎

(6.5) Remark.

Assume
S:=\mathop{\rm Spec}(k)
where
k
is any algebraically closed field, and

K
= k. Then (
6.4
), statement and proof, recover and extend
the result of
[
Trento
]
*(2.8), (2.10), p. 148: it asserts, in the
setup of (
6.4
), that the map
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}_{U}}^{\mathbf{h}}\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}_{U}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
is an isomorphism
locally at
(\mathscr{B},\,\mathscr{C})
if
\mathscr{A}
is Gorenstein. Of
course,(
6.4
) proves
\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}
is an isomorphism
globally on
U
without assuming
\mathscr{A}_{U}
is Gorenstein; moreover,
(
6.4
)(1) asserts a more general result where
\mathscr{B}
needn’t be
the universal sheaf
\mathscr{B}^{\spadesuit}
. (However,
[
Trento
]
treats
non-Artinian
\mathscr{B}
and
\mathscr{C}
as well.)

Also, (
6.4
)(2) recovers the last assertion in

[
PGor
]
*Prop. 1.7, p. 611, and extends it to an
\mathscr{A}
that
isn’t a polynomial ring. In both proofs, the key is the smoothness of
the forgetful map
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}}\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{b},\,\mathbf{h}}\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
at
(\mathscr{B},\,\mathscr{C})
, but in (
6.4
)
the proof of the key is more direct. Note that the proof of Prop. 1.7
doesn’t use the blanket hypothesis that
\mathop{\rm char}(k)\neq 2
made on
p. 607; thus Prop. 1.7 holds in arbitrary characteristic.

Set
B:=\Gamma(\mathscr{B})
. Then Prop. 1.7 of
[
PGor
]
also asserts
the following useful formula:

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathscr{C}}\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}

\displaystyle+\dim_{k}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{B}\bigl(J/J^{2},\,I\,\bigr)_{0}

\displaystyle=\dim_{\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C})}\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}+\dim_{k}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{B}\bigl(J/J^{2},\,I^{\prime}\bigr)_{0}.

(6.5.1)

where
J
,
I
and
I^{\prime}
are the ideals of
B
in
\Gamma(\mathscr{A})
, of

\Gamma(\mathscr{C})
in
B
and of
\Gamma({\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C})})
in
B
.
Since
J
is generated by a regular sequence,
J/J^{2}
is a free

B
-module, and so the two Hom’s can be easy to compute.

An infinitesimal analysis, made in
[
PGor
]
, shows the Hom’s in
(
6.5
) are the tangent spaces to the fibers of
\varphi^{h}
and

\varphi^{h^{\prime}}
at
(\mathscr{B},\,\mathscr{C})
and at
(\mathscr{B},\,\lambda^{\mathbf{h}}(\mathscr{C}))
. But, at those points,
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}}
and
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
are
smooth. Thus the local dimensions of their fibers are the two

\dim_{k}
’s. In general, the dimension of a fiber over a point can be
computed by base change to the algebraic closure of the point’s residue
field. Thus those two
\dim_{k}
’s are the local dimensions of the fibers
of
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}}
and
\varphi^{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}}
in the generality of
(
6.4
)(2).

A good example of the use of (
6.4
)(2) and (
6.5
),
with the aid of Macaulay 2, was made in
[
LevAlg
]
*Ex. 49, p. 697
to give an affirmative answer to Iarrobino’s Ques. 1.3ii on p. 276 in

[
Pen2005
]
: Is there an
\mathbf{h}
such that
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, where
A
is a polynomial ring in three variables, has two
different components whose general quotients are level of type 2?
(Other such
\mathbf{h}
were found independently by Boij and Iarrobino, but
left unpublished according to the abstract of
[
RedLev
]
). In

[
LevAlg
]
, one component is generically smooth of dimension 47, as a
certain quotient can be connected via a sequence of direct linkages to a
quotient whose ideal is generated by a certain regular sequence. The
second component is generically smooth of dimension 46, as was shown by
direct computation and also by conceptual means.

(6.6) Lemma.

Fix an
S
-map
T\to R
, and a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{R}
-module
\mathscr{K}
.

(1)
Then
\mathscr{K}_{T}
vanishes iff
T\to R
factors through
U:=R-\mathop{\rm Supp}(\mathscr{K})
.

(2)
Given a surjection
\lambda\colon\mathscr{L}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{M}
of coherent
\mathscr{O}_{R}
-modules, where
\mathscr{M}
is flat, take
\mathscr{K}:=\mathop{\rm Ker}\lambda
. Then

\lambda_{T}\colon\mathscr{L}_{T}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathscr{M}_{T}
iff
T\to R
factors through
U:=R-\mathop{\rm Supp}(\mathscr{K})
.

Proof.

For (1),
given a (scheme) point
t\in T
, denote by
r\in R
its image,
and by
k(r)/k(t)
the extension of residue fields. By Nakayama’s
Lemma, the stalk
\mathscr{K}_{T,t}
vanishes iff the fiber
\mathscr{K}_{T,t}\otimes k(t)
vanishes; similarly,
\mathscr{K}_{r}
vanishes iff
\mathscr{K}_{r}\otimes k(r)

vanishes. But
\mathscr{K}_{r}\otimes k(r)
vanishes iff
r\in U
. Thus (1) holds.

For (2),
note that
0\to\mathscr{K}\to\mathscr{L}\xrightarrow{\lambda}\mathscr{M}\to 0

remains exact on pullback to
T
as
\mathscr{M}
is flat. So
\lambda_{T}\colon\mathscr{L}_{T}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathscr{M}_{T}
iff
\mathscr{K}_{T}
vanishes. Thus (1) implies (2).
∎

(6.7) Proposition.

As the function
\mathbf{t}\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}
varies, the functors
T\mapsto\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
are representable by (possibly empty)
subschemes, say
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
, of
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
stratifying it, and the
T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
are representable by the intersections
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}:=\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}\cap\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, which stratify
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
.
Further,
T\mapsto\mathbf{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
and
T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
are representable, say by
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
and
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
; in fact,

\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is an open subscheme of
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, and
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}=\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}\cap\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
.

Proof.

Set
R:=\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
for short. Let
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}^{\mathbf{h}}
denote the universal quotient. Set
\mathscr{R}:=\mathscr{C}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{R}}\mathscr{O}_{R}
. It is easy to adapt the construction
of
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
in the proof of (
6.2
) with
R

and
\mathscr{R}
in place of
Q
and
\mathscr{Q}
to construct
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
for each
\mathbf{t}
. Plainly, every scheme-point of
R

lies in some
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
, because, for every

T/S
where
T
is the Spec of a field,
\mathscr{C}_{T}
has some
T
-socle type

\mathbf{t}
; thus, the
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
stratify
R
.

Recall from (
6.3
) that
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is a
(closed) subscheme of
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. So the
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
are well defined. Plainly, they represent
the
T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
and stratify
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
.

Further, therefore,
T\mapsto\mathbf{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
and

T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
are representable, say by

\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
and
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, and
also
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}=\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}\cap\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, as
\mathbf{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}=\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
and
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}=\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
where
\mathbf{t}(s):=\mathbf{h}(s)
, and
\mathbf{t}(p):=0
for
p\neq s
; see the end of (
6.1
).

Here’s an alternative construction of
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
,
which, moreover, shows that it’s an open subscheme of
R
. Given an

S
-map
T\to R
, notice
\mathscr{C}_{T}\in\mathbb{AF}_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. Also,
there’s a canonical surjection
\lambda(T)\colon\mathscr{R}_{T}\twoheadrightarrow G_{-s}(\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*})
owing to (
4.4
)(1). And
\lambda(T)
is an
isomorphism iff
\mathscr{C}_{T}
is, by definition (
4.5
), level.

Note
\mathscr{C}^{*}\in\mathbb{AF}_{\mathscr{A}_{R}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
by (
2.7
)(4); so

G_{-s}(\mathscr{C}^{*})
is flat. Also,
\lambda(T)=\lambda(R)_{T}
by
(
4.10
)(4), (2). Set
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}:=R-\mathop{\rm Supp}(\mathop{\rm Ker}\lambda(R))
. Then
\mathbf{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is,
by (
6.6
)(2), the set of
T
-points of
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, which is an open subscheme of
R:=\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
,
as desired.
∎

(6.8)  (Another construction).

Set
t:=\mathbf{h}(s)
. Note
0<t\leq\mathbf{b}(s)
by (
6.1
). Set

\mathbb{G}:=\mathop{\mathbb{G}\rm rass}\nolimits_{t}(\mathscr{B}_{s})
, the Grassmannian of rank-
t
locally
free quotients of
\mathscr{B}_{s}
. Let
\mathscr{U}
be the universal quotient of

(\mathscr{B}_{s})_{\mathbb{G}}
on
\mathbb{G}
; so
\mathscr{U}^{*}\subset(\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger})_{-s}
. Form the natural map:

\mu_{p}\colon(\mathscr{A}_{\mathbb{G}})_{p+s}\otimes\mathscr{U}^{*}\to(\mathscr{B}_{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger})_{p}\text{\quad for\kern 5.0pt}p\geq-s.

(6.8.1)

Denote by
L_{p}\subset\mathbb{G}
the subscheme where
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mu_{p})=\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
; that is, an
S
-map
T\to\mathbb{G}
factors through
L_{p}
iff

\mathop{\rm Cok}((\mu_{p})_{T})
is locally free of rank
\mathbf{b}^{*}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
.
But, by right exactness of pullback,
\mathop{\rm Cok}((\mu_{p})_{T})=(\mathop{\rm Cok}(\mu_{p}))_{T}
. So by the theory of flattening stratification,
L_{p}

exists, although it might be empty. Set

\textstyle\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}:=\bigcap_{p=-s}^{-i(\mathbf{h})}L_{p}\subset\mathbb{G}\text{\quad where }i(\mathbf{h}):=\inf\,\{\,p\mid\mathbf{h}(p)\neq 0\,\}.

In passing, notice, if
\mathscr{A}_{p+s}
is locally free, say of rank
\mathbf{a}(p+s)
, then
\mu_{p}
is locally represented by a
\mathbf{b}^{*}(p)
by
t\mathbf{a}(p+s)
matrix. So locally
L_{p}
is defined by the vanishing of its
minors of size
\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)+1
and the nonvanishing of those size
\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
; moreover,

\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)\leq\min\{\,t\,\mathbf{a}(p+s),\ \mathbf{b}^{*}(p)\,\}.

(6.8.2)

Given a scheme point
g\in\mathbb{G}
, let
K
be its residue field. Then

g\in\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
iff the map of
K
-vector spaces

\mu_{p}\otimes K
is of rank
\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
for
p\geq-s
. Thus as
\mathbf{h}

varies, the
\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
stratify
\mathbb{G}
; that is,
set-theoretically their disjoint union
\coprod_{h}\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is
\mathbb{G}
.

Assume
S
is reduced and irreducible. Assume
L:=\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
contains the generic point of
\mathbb{G}
. Then
L
is open,
since it’s the intersection of an open subscheme and a closed subscheme,
and the latter must be all of
\mathbb{G}
. Thus
L
is nonempty, reduced,
and irreducible, and it’s covered by open subschemes, each one
isomorphic to an open subscheme of the affine space over
S
of fiber
dimension
t(\mathbf{b}(s)-t)
. Also, by lower semicontinuity of rank, if

\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}}\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}(p)\leq\mathbf{h}(p)
for all
p
. Notice
\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}(p)\leq\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}(p)
, with
equality iff
\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}=\mathbf{h}
. So call
\mathbf{h}

maximal
for

\mathscr{B}
and
t
.

(6.9) Remark.

In one important case, let’s see that the map
\mu_{p}
of (
6.8.1
)
can be represented locally by a “catalecticant” matrix; cf.
[
MaxGor
]
*Thm. 11, proof, p. 3146.

Take
t:=1
. Then
\mathbb{G}=\mathop{\mathbb{P}}(\mathscr{B}_{s})
and
\mathscr{U}^{*}=\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{G}}(-1)\subset(\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{\mathbb{G}})_{-s}
.

Assume
S:=\mathop{\rm Spec}(k)
for some (Noetherian) ring
k
. Let
X
be a
vector of
r
weighted variables, set
A:=k[X]
and take
\mathscr{B}:=\mathscr{A}:=\widetilde{A}
. Then
A_{s}
is the
k
-span of the monomials
X^{w}
where

w
is a multiindex of weight
s
. As in (
3.7
), view

(A^{\dagger})_{-s}
as the
k
-span of the Laurent monomials
X^{-w}
.

Let
R
denote the polynomial ring over
k
on the
X^{w}
viewed as
independent variables. Then
\mathbb{G}=\mathop{\rm Proj}(R)
. Moreover, the
universal surjection
(\mathscr{A}_{s})_{\mathbb{G}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{U}
arises from the
tautological
R
-map
R\otimes_{k}A_{s}\to R[1]
sending
1\otimes X^{w}
to
X^{w}
in

R_{1}=A_{s}
. So the inclusion
\mathscr{U}^{*}\hookrightarrow(\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}_{s})_{\mathbb{G}}

arises from the
R
-dual map
R[-1]\to R\otimes_{k}(A^{\dagger})_{-s}
sending
1 to
\sum X^{w}\otimes X^{-w}
in
R_{1}\otimes_{k}(A^{\dagger})_{-s}
, because the
canonical isomorphism
A_{s}\otimes_{k}(A^{\dagger})_{-s}=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{k}(A_{s},\,A_{s})

identifies
\sum X^{w}\otimes X^{-w}
with 1.

Fix
w_{0}
, and set
x_{w}:=X^{w}/X^{w_{0}}
. Then the principal open set

D_{+}(X^{w_{0}})
is equal to
\mathop{\rm Spec}(k[\{x_{w}\}])
. Fix an affine

S
-scheme
T:=\mathop{\rm Spec}(K)
and a
T
-point of
D_{+}(X^{w_{0}})
with
coordinates
(a_{w})
; the latter is the
S
-map
T\to D_{+}(X^{w})
defined
by
x_{w}\mapsto a_{w}
. Then
\mathscr{U}^{*}_{T}=\mathscr{O}_{T}\cdot f\subset(\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}_{T})_{-s}
where
f:=\sum a_{w}X^{-w}
in
(A^{\dagger}_{K})_{-s}=\Gamma((\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}_{T})_{-s})
.

Note that
\mu_{p}
arises from the map
(A_{K})_{p+s}\to(A_{K}^{\dagger})_{p}

of multiplication by
f
for
p\geq-s
. As bases of
(A_{K})_{p+s}
and

(A_{K}^{\dagger})_{p}
, take the monomials
X^{v}
and
X^{-u}
of weights

p+s
and
p
. Then
\mu_{p}
is represented by the “catalecticant”
matrix
((b_{uv}))
where
b_{uv}:=a_{w}
with
w:=u+v
.

(6.10) Theorem.

The scheme
\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
of (
6.8
) represents

T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
; in other words, if
nonempty,
\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
carries a universal level
homogeneous quotient,
\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
say.

Proof.

Given
T
, we have to see that the set of
T
-points of
\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is equal to
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. In
particular, taking
T:=\mathbb{HL}_{B}^{\mathbf{h}}
yields
\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
.

Given a map
T\to\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, set
\mathscr{D}_{p}:=\mathop{\rm Im}((\mu_{p})_{T})
and
\mathscr{D}:=\bigoplus_{p\geq-s}\mathscr{D}_{p}
. Plainly,

\mathscr{D}
is an
\mathscr{A}_{T}
-submodule of
(\mathscr{B}^{\dagger})_{T}
. Also,
0\to\mathscr{D}_{p}\to(\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})_{p}\to\mathop{\rm Cok}((\mu_{p})_{T})\to 0
is exact. But

\mathop{\rm Cok}((\mu_{p})_{T})
is locally free of rank
\mathbf{b}^{*}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
. So

\mathscr{D}_{p}
is locally free of rank
\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
. Thus
\mathscr{D}\in\mathbb{AG}_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
.

Note
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{T}/\mathscr{D}=\bigl(\bigoplus_{p<-s}(\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})_{p}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(\bigoplus_{p\geq-s}\mathop{\rm Cok}((\mu_{p})_{T})\bigr)
. So
\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}/\mathscr{D}
is flat. Thus
\mathscr{D}\in\mathbf{H}\Delta_{\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
. Set
\mathscr{C}:=\mathscr{D}^{*}
. Then
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{B_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
and
\mathscr{C}^{*}=\mathscr{D}
by (
3.5
). But
\mathscr{D}
is generated
by
\mathscr{D}_{-s}
by definition of
\mu_{p}
. So
\mathscr{C}
is level. Thus

\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
.

Conversely, given
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, set

\mathscr{D}:=\mathscr{C}^{*}
. As
\mathscr{C}
is level,
\mathscr{D}_{-s}

generates
\mathscr{D}
. Also,
\mathscr{D}\in\mathbf{H}\Delta_{B_{T}^{\dagger}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
by (
3.5
); hence,
(\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})_{p}/\mathscr{D}_{p}
is locally
free of rank
\mathbf{b}^{*}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
.

Note that
\mathscr{D}_{-s}
is locally free of rank
t
as
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. Hence there’s a unique map
T\to\mathbb{G}

with
\mathscr{U}_{T}=\mathscr{C}_{s}
. So
(\mathscr{U}_{T})^{*}=\mathscr{D}_{-s}
. But
\mathscr{D}_{-s}
generates
\mathscr{D}
. So
\mathop{\rm Im}(\mu_{p})_{T}=\mathscr{D}_{p}
. So

\mathop{\rm Cok}((\mu_{p})_{T})
is locally free of rank
\mathbf{b}^{*}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
.
Thus
T\to\mathbb{G}
factors through all
L_{p}
. Thus the set of
T
-points
of
\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is equal to
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
.
∎

(6.11) Corollary.

There’s a canonical isomorphism of schemes:
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}=\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
.

Proof.

By (
6.7
) and (
6.10
), both
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}

and
\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
represent the functor
T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. Thus
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
and

\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
are canonically isomorphic.
∎

7.
Schemes of Multilevel Homogeneous Quotients

(7.1) Setup.

Keep the setup of (
6.1
) with this exception: don’t fix the
“potential Hilbert function”
\mathbf{h}
a priori; so don’t set
s:=s(\mathbf{h})
, but define
s
as in (3) below. Note however that, given a

filtered
S
-Artinian
\mathscr{A}
-module with Hilbert function
\mathbf{h}
and

S
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
, plainly (
4.4
)(2) yields
s(\mathbf{h})=s(\mathbf{t})
.

Assume
\mathscr{A}/F^{1}\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{O}_{S}
. Fix a “socle type,” a nonzero
finite function
\mathbf{t}\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}
.

With (
4.13
)–(
4.15
) in mind, call a set
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}

of functions
fit
for
\mathbf{t}
if

(1)

\mathbf{t}(p)=\mathbf{h}_{p}(p)-\mathbf{h}_{p+1}(p)
for all

p
, and

(2)

\mathbf{b}(p)\geq\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)\geq\mathbf{h}_{m+1}(p)\geq 0
for all
m
and
p
, and

(3)

\mathbf{h}_{m}=0
for all
m>s:=s(\mathbf{t}):=\sup\{\,p\mid\mathbf{t}(p)>0\,\}
.

Notice that (1) and (2) imply
\mathbf{b}(p)\geq\mathbf{t}(p)\geq 0
for all
p
.

Set
\bar{s}:=i(\mathbf{t}):=\inf\,\{\,p\mid\mathbf{t}(p)\neq 0\,\}
. To

facilitate recursion on
(s-\bar{s})
, set

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(p):=\begin{cases}0&\text{for }p=\bar{s},\\
\mathbf{t}(p)&\text{for }p\neq\bar{s},\end{cases}\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}:=\begin{cases}\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}&\text{for }m\leq\bar{s}+1,\\
\mathbf{h}_{m}&\text{for }m\geq\bar{s}+1.\end{cases}

Call
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
the
attendants
to
\mathbf{t}
and

\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
. Note
\mathbf{t}
and
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
are fixed through out this
section, but
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
are not.

Suppose temporarily
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
is fit for
\mathbf{t}
. If
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}=0
,
or equivalently
\bar{s}=s
, note that (3) yields
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}=0
for all

m
. If
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\neq 0
, or equivalently
\bar{s}<s
, set
\bar{s}^{\prime}:=i(\mathbf{t}^{\prime})
and note that
\bar{s}<\bar{s}^{\prime}\leq s
and that
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
is fit for

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
.

Finally, generalize the notion of multilevel module in (
4.13
) to
sheaves, and for any
T/S
and any
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
, denote the sets of all
multilevel graded and all multilevel filtered quotients of
\mathscr{B}_{T}

with the
\mathbf{h}_{m}
as Hilbert functions by
\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
and
\mathbf{F\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
.

Similarly, generalize the operator
\Delta^{n}
and
\Lambda^{n}
of
(
4.13
) to sheaves
\mathscr{D}\in\mathbf{H\Delta}_{B^{\dagger}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}^{*}\}}
and
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
. By
(
4.14
)(1), all these objects are functorial in
T
.

If
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
is fit for
\mathbf{t}
, then
\mathbf{t}(p)=\mathbf{h}_{p}(p)-\mathbf{h}_{p+1}(p)
by (1); so by (
4.14
)(2), for any
T/S
, every
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
is of
T
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
.
The converse holds, as noted below.

Assume
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
is of
T
-socle
type
\mathbf{t}
. Then
\mathscr{C}=\Lambda^{m}\mathscr{C}
for
m\leq\bar{s}
by
(
4.13
) as
\mathscr{C}^{*}
is locally generated in degree at most

-\bar{s}
. So
\mathbf{h}_{m}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}
for
m\leq\bar{s}
, and
\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}
is the Hilbert function of
\mathscr{C}
. Similarly,
\mathscr{C}_{p}=0
for
p>s
as
\mathscr{C}^{*}
is locally generated in degree at least

-s
. Thus
s=s(\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}})
. And
\mathbf{b}(p)=0
for
p<{0}
by
(
6.1
); thus

\textstyle\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C})=\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(p)\leq\sum_{p=0}^{s}\mathbf{b}(p).

(7.1.1)

Note
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
is fit for
\mathbf{t}
, since (
4.14
)(2) gives
(1), and (2)–(3) follow from (
4.13
). So
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
is fit
for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
by the above. Also,
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}
by (
4.14
)(3) with
n:=\bar{s}+1
. Thus
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}
is of
T
-socle type
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
.

(7.2) Proposition.

Fix
\mathbf{h}\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}
. As
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
varies keeping
\mathbf{h}_{0}=\mathbf{h}
, the functors
T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
and
T\mapsto\mathbf{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
are
representable by (possibly empty) subschemes
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
and
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
and
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, stratifying
them. Moreover,
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
is the preimage of

\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
under the retraction
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
.

Proof.

Set
H:=\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
, and let
\mathscr{D}\subset\mathscr{B}_{H}^{\dagger}
be the universal subsheaf with Hilbert function
\mathbf{h}^{*}
.
Much as in the proof of (
6.2
), the theory of flattening
stratification yields, for every
n
, a subscheme
H_{m}
of
H
such that
an
S
-map
T\to H
factors through
H_{m}
iff
\Delta^{n}\mathscr{D}_{T}\in\mathbf{H\Delta}_{\mathscr{D}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}_{m}^{*}}
. Set
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}:=\bigcap_{m}H_{m}
. Then
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}

represents
T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
. Plainly,
every scheme-point of
H
lies in
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}

for some choice of
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
. Thus, the
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
stratify
H
. Finally, it’s immediate from the
definitions that the preimage of
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
in

\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
represents the functor
T\mapsto\mathbf{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
.
∎

(7.3)  (A recursive construction).

Fix
\{\mathbf{h}_{n}\}
, and let’s recursively construct a scheme
L:=\mathbb{HL}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}
, and if
L\neq\emptyset
, also a
tautological multilevel homogeneous quotient
\mathscr{L}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}
in
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{L}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
of
L
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
. If
L=\emptyset
, let’s leave
\mathscr{L}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}

undefined.

If there exist a
T/S
and a
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
, then
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
is fit for
\mathbf{t}
if
\mathscr{C}
is of

T
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
, or equivalently, if
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
satisfies
(
7.1
)(1); see the end of (
7.1
). If not, set
L:=\emptyset
. So let’s assume
\{\mathbf{h}_{n}\}
is fit for
\mathbf{t}
, and let

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
be their attendants. Recall that either

\bar{s}=s
or
\bar{s}<\bar{s}^{\prime}\leq s
. Let’s proceed by recursion on

(s-\bar{s})

If
\bar{s}=s
, set
L^{\prime}:=S
and
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}=0
. If
\bar{s}<s
, set
L^{\prime}:=\mathbb{HL}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}
, and if
L^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
, set
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{L}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}
; by recursion,
L^{\prime}
and
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
are
defined. If
L^{\prime}=\emptyset
, set
L:=\mathbb{HL}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}:=\emptyset
.

Assume
L^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
. Set
\overline{\mathbf{h}}:=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}-\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}
. Set
\mathscr{B}^{\prime}:=\mathop{\rm Ker}(\mathscr{B}_{L^{\prime}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{L}^{\prime})
, and let
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}
be its Hilbert function. Now,
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
has Hilbert function
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}^{\prime}}
. But
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}^{\prime}}=\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}
as
\bar{s}+1\leq\bar{s}^{\prime}
.
And
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}
by definition of the
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}
.
So
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}=\mathbf{b}-\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}
. Thus
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}(p)\geq\overline{\mathbf{h}}(p)\geq 0
for all
p
. So using (
6.8
), define

L:=\mathbb{HL}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}:=L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}.

(7.3.1)

If
L\neq\emptyset
, define
\mathscr{L}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}
via the
following pushout diagram on
L
:

\kern 5.0pt\kern-2.5pt0\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to19.32098pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\mathscr{B}^{\prime}_{L}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to24.49521pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\mathscr{B}_{L}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to23.50735pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.83313pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}0

\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$}}\Big\downarrow\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle$\hss}}

\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$}}\Big\downarrow\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle$\hss}}

\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$}}\Big\downarrow\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle 1$\hss}}

\kern 5.0pt\kern-5.0pt\ 0\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.83313pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.83313pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\mathscr{L}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.83313pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.83313pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}0

(7.3.2)

Note
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
is in
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{L^{\prime}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}
. So

\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}
is in
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{L}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}
by
(
4.14
)(1). By construction,
\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
is in

\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{L}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
. Thus (
4.15
) yields

\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{L}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
, as desired.

(7.4) Theorem.

The scheme
L:=\mathbb{HL}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}
of (
7.3
)
represents
T\mapsto\smash{\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}}
, and
if
L\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
is the
universal multilevel homogeneous quotient of
\mathscr{B}_{L}
.

Proof.

If
L\neq\emptyset
, set
\mathscr{L}:=\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
.
Given
T\neq\emptyset
and a multilevel sheaf
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
, we have to show
L\neq\emptyset
and find a unique map
\tau\colon T\to L
with
\mathscr{C}=\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}
. To do so, as in (
7.3
), let’s proceed by recursion on

(s-\bar{s})
.

First, suppose
L\neq\emptyset
and
\tau
exists; let’s check
\tau

is unique. Note
L^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
as
L\neq\emptyset
. Let

\lambda\colon L\to L^{\prime}
be the structure map, and set
\tau^{\prime}:=\lambda\tau
. So
\tau^{\prime}\colon T\to L^{\prime}
.

If
\bar{s}=s
, then
L^{\prime}=S
, and so
\tau^{\prime}\colon T\to S
is the structure
map, so unique.

Assume
\bar{s}<s
. Then by recursion
\tau^{\prime*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
determines
\tau^{\prime}
.
But
\tau^{\prime*}\mathscr{L}=\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}
. By construction,
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}=\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{L}
. So
\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}=\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}(\tau^{*}\mathscr{L})=\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}
. So
\mathscr{C}
determines
\tau^{\prime}
.

For any
\bar{s}
, view
\tau
as a lifting of
\tau^{\prime}
. Then
\tau
is
determined by the level quotient
\tau^{*}\overline{\mathscr{L}}
of
\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\prime}
by
(
6.10
). Pull back (
7.3.2
) under
\tau
. The resulting
diagram plainly has surjective vertical maps, and as
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}
is
flat, it also has exact rows. But, as was just observed, the surjection

\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}\twoheadrightarrow\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}
is equal to
\mathscr{C}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}
. But
\tau^{*}\overline{\mathscr{L}}
is the kernel of
\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}\twoheadrightarrow\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}
. Hence
\tau^{*}\overline{\mathscr{L}}
is determined by
\mathscr{C}
. Thus
\tau
is too, as desired.

It remains to show
L\neq\emptyset
and find
\tau
. Set
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}:=\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}
. Proceed by recursion.

If
\bar{s}=s
, then
L^{\prime}=S
. So define
\tau^{\prime}\colon T\to L^{\prime}
to be the
structure map.

Assume
\bar{s}<s
. Then
C^{\prime}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}

by (
4.14
)(3) with
n:=\bar{s}+1
. So by recursion,
L^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
and there’s a map
\tau^{\prime}\colon T\to L^{\prime}
with
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
.

For any
\bar{s}
, set
\overline{\mathscr{C}}=\mathop{\rm Ker}(\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}^{\prime})
. Then
\overline{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
by (
4.14
)(4) with
n:=\bar{s}+1
. So
L\neq\emptyset
and
\tau^{\prime}
lifts to a map
\tau\colon T\to L

with
\overline{\mathscr{C}}=\tau^{*}\overline{\mathscr{L}}
by (
6.10
). Pull back
(
7.3.2
) under
\tau
. As noted above, the resulting diagram
has exact rows and surjective vertical maps. But
\overline{\mathscr{C}}=\tau^{*}\overline{\mathscr{L}}
and
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}=\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{L}
. Thus
\mathscr{C}=\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}
, as
desired.
∎

(7.5) Corollary.

There’s a canonical isomorphism:
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}=\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
.

Proof.

By (
7.2
) and (
7.4
), both
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
and
\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
represent

T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}}
. Thus
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
and
\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
are
canonically isomorphic.
∎

(7.6)  (Recursively maximal).

Call a set
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}

recursively maximal
for
\mathbf{t}
and

T/S
if these three conditions hold, with
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}

the attendants to
\mathbf{t}
and
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
:

(1)

if
\bar{s}<s
, then
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
is recursively maximal
for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
T/S
;

(2)

given any
T^{\diamond}/T
and any
\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}
and any
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\diamond}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}}
of
T^{\diamond}
-socle
type
\mathbf{t}
, with
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\diamond}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}
if
\bar{s}<s
, necessarily
\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)
for all
m,\ p
;

(3)

there exist
T^{\sharp}/T
and
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\sharp}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
of
T^{\sharp}
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
.

Given (1) and (3), the inequality
\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)
in (2)
holds for all
m
if it holds just for
m=\bar{s}
. Indeed, (1)
implies it holds for
m>\bar{s}
by recursion. But both
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}

and
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
are of socle type
\mathbf{t}
; so
\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}=\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{\bar{s}}
and
\mathbf{h}_{m}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}
for
m\leq\bar{s}
by the
end of (
7.1
).

Notice, in (2), it suffices to use
T^{\diamond}
of the form
\mathop{\rm Spec}K
where

K
is a field. Indeed, given any
T^{\diamond}
, just take
K
to be one of
its residue fields. Ditto for (3).

Notice (3) implies
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
is fit for
\mathbf{t}
by the end of
(
7.1
).

Notice, if
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}
are both recursively
maximal for
\mathbf{t}
and
T/S
, then
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}
.
Indeed, let
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\diamond}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}}
be provided by (3). Now, if
\bar{s}<s
, then
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
is equal to the attendant set to
\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}
by (1) and
recursion; hence,
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\diamond}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}
by (
4.14
)(3) with
n:=\bar{s}+1
.
So
\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)
for all
m
and
p
by (2). By
symmetry,
\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}(p)
for all
m
and
p
. Thus

\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
.

(7.7)  (Quasi-permissible).

Call
\mathbf{t}

quasi-permissible
if either
\bar{s}=s
and then

\mathbf{b}(s)\geq\mathbf{t}(s)
, or else
\bar{s}<s
and then these two conditions
hold:

(1)

the attendant
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
to
\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible;

(2)

given any
T^{\flat}/S
and any
\{\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{m}\}
recursively maximal for

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
T^{\flat}
, necessarily

\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\geq\mathbf{t}(\bar{s}).

(7.8) Theorem.

If there exists a recursively maximal set for
\mathbf{t}
and
T/S
, then

\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible. If
\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible and
S

is reduced and irreducible, then there exists a recursively maximal set

\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
for
\mathbf{t}
and
T/S
; moreover, for any such set
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
, then
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{B}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
is nonempty, reduced, and
irreducible, and it’s covered by open subschemes, with each one
isomorphic to an open subscheme of the affine space over
S
of fiber
dimension
\mathbf{H}
where
\mathbf{H}:=\sum_{p}\mathbf{t}(p)\bigl(\mathbf{b}(p)-\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(p)\bigr)
.

Proof.

First, assume there exists a recursively maximal set
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
for

\mathbf{t}
and
T/S
. Note (
7.6
)(3) provides
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\sharp}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
of
T^{\sharp}
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
. So
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})\geq\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(\bar{s})
, and
\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})
by (
4.14
)(2).
Thus
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\geq\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})
.

Suppose
\bar{s}=s
. Then
\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}=0
by (
4.13
).
Thus
\mathbf{b}(s)\geq\mathbf{t}(s)
as required by (
7.7
).

Suppose
\bar{s}<s
. Then
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
is recursively maximal for

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
T/S
by (
7.6
)(1). So by recursion,
(
7.7
)(1) holds. Let’s prove (
7.7
)(2). Note

\{\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
by the last paragraph of
(
7.6
). But
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}
. Thus

\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{\bar{s}+1}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}
, as desired.

Next, assume
\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible and
S
is reduced and
irreducible. Roughly follow (
7.3
), proceeding by recursion on

(s-\bar{s})
. Set
\overline{t}:=\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})
.

Suppose
\bar{s}=s
. Again set
L^{\prime}:=S
, set
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}=0
, and set

\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}:=0
for all
m
. Then
L^{\prime}
is nonempty, reduced, and
irreducible by hypothesis; trivially, it’s of fiber dimension 0. As

\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}:=0
and
\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible,
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\geq\overline{t}>0
.

Suppose
\bar{s}<s
. By recursion assume that there’s a recursively
maximal set
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
S
, and that
L^{\prime}:=\mathbb{HL}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}
is nonempty, reduced, and irreducible,
and covered by open subschemes, each one isomorphic to an open subscheme
of the affine space over
S
of fiber dimension
\mathbf{H}^{\prime}
where
\mathbf{H}^{\prime}:=\sum_{p}\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(p)\bigl(\mathbf{b}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}^{\prime}}(p)\bigr)
. So
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}=\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}^{\prime}}
for
m\leq\bar{s}^{\prime}
by (
4.13
) with

\mathscr{L}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}
for
\mathscr{C}
. But
\bar{s}+1\leq\bar{s}^{\prime}
. Thus
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}=\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}
for
m\leq\bar{s}+1
.
Also,
\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible; so (
7.7
)(2) gives
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\geq\overline{t}>0
.

For any
\bar{s}
, set
\mathscr{B}^{\prime}:=\mathop{\rm Ker}(\mathscr{B}_{L^{\prime}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{L}^{\prime})
; let
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}
be its Hilbert function. Now,
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
has Hilbert function
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}^{\prime}}
. But
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}^{\prime}}=\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}
and
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\geq\overline{t}>0
. Thus
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}=\mathbf{b}-\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}
, and so
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}(\bar{s})\geq\overline{t}>0
. Hence, the
end of (
6.8
) with
S:=L^{\prime}
yields a maximal Hilbert function,

\bar{\mathbf{h}}
say, for
\mathscr{B}^{\prime}
and
\overline{t}
. For use below, note that the
construction in (
6.8
) yields
\bar{t}=\bar{\mathbf{h}}(\bar{s})
.

Set
\mathbf{h}_{m}:=\overline{\mathbf{h}}+\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}
for
m\leq\bar{s}
; set
\mathbf{h}_{m}:=\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}
for
m>\bar{s}
. Recall
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}=\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}
for

m\leq\bar{s}+1
; see above. Hence
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
is the attendant to

\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
. Thus (
7.6
)(1) holds.

The end of (
6.8
) also yields a nonempty, reduced and irreducible

L:=\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
. By (
6.10
), it represents

T\mapsto\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
, and correspondingly
carries a universal quotient
\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
.
Set
\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}:=L
and
\mathscr{L}:=\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
.

Given any
T^{\diamond}/S
and any
\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}
and any
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\diamond}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}}
of
T^{\diamond}
-socle type

\mathbf{t}
, with
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\diamond}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}
if
\bar{s}<s
, there’s a unique map
\tau^{\prime}\colon T^{\diamond}\to L^{\prime}
with
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}=\tau^{\prime*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
. Also,

\tau^{\prime}
lifts to a unique map
\tau\colon T^{\diamond}\to L
with
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}=\tau^{*}\mathscr{L}
. But
\overline{\mathbf{h}}
is maximal. So
\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{\bar{s}}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{\bar{s}+1}(p)\leq\overline{\mathbf{h}}(p)
for all
p
. Thus
\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{\bar{s}}(p)\leq\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(p)
for all
p
. Thus
(
7.6
)(2) holds. And, if
T^{\diamond}:=L
and
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}:=\mathscr{L}
, then
\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}=\mathbf{h}_{m}
for all
m
; thus (
7.6
)(3)
holds.

By the end of (
6.8
) too,
L
is covered by open subschemes, each
one isomorphic to an open subscheme of the affine space over
L^{\prime}
of
fiber dimension
\overline{t}(\mathbf{b}^{\prime}(\bar{s})-\overline{t})
. From the above, recall
\mathbf{b}^{\prime}=\mathbf{b}-\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(\bar{s}):=\overline{\mathbf{h}}(\bar{s})+\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})
and
\overline{t}=\overline{\mathbf{h}}(\bar{s})

and
\overline{t}:=\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})
. Thus
\overline{t}(\mathbf{b}^{\prime}(\bar{s})-\overline{t})=\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})\bigl(\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(\bar{s}))
.

However,
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(p)=0
for
p\leq\bar{s}
, and
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(p)=\mathbf{t}(p)

for
p>\bar{s}
; furthermore,
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}^{\prime}}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}
, and
\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}+1}(p)=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(p)
for
p>\bar{s}
; see (
7.1
) and
(
4.13
). Hence

\textstyle\overline{t}(\mathbf{b}^{\prime}(\bar{s})-\overline{t})+\sum_{p}\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(p)\bigl(\mathbf{b}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}^{\prime}}(p)\bigr)=\sum_{p}\mathbf{t}(p)\bigl(\mathbf{b}(p)-\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(p)\bigr).

Thus
L
is covered by open subschemes, each one isomorphic to an open
subscheme of the affine space over
S
of fiber dimension
\mathbf{H}
, as
desired.
∎

(7.9)  (Recursively compressed).

Given
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
for some

\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
and some
T/S
, call
\mathscr{C}

recursively compressed
for

\mathbf{t}
if
\mathscr{C}
is of
T
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
and if the
following two conditions hold, where
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
is the attendant to
\mathbf{t}
:

(1)

if
\bar{s}<s
, then
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}
is recursively
compressed for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
;

(2)

for any
T^{\diamond}/S
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}
and
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\diamond}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}}
of
T^{\diamond}
-socle type

\mathbf{t}
, with
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}
recursively compressed for

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
if
\bar{s}<s
, necessarily
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\diamond})\leq\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C})
.

Notice, for every recursively compressed
\mathscr{C}
for
\mathbf{t}
, the number

\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C})
is the same. Moreover, if
\mathscr{C}
is recursively
compressed, then so is
\mathscr{C}_{R}
for any
R/T
.

(7.10) Theorem.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible.

(1)
In some
\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
, there exists a
recursively compressed
\mathscr{C}
for
\mathbf{t}
.

(2)
Let
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
be recursively maximal for
\mathbf{t}
and some

T/S
. Let
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\in\smash{\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\sharp}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}\}}}
be of
T^{\sharp}
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
. Then
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}\}
iff
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
is recursively compressed for
\mathbf{t}
.

Proof.

Let’s prove (1) and (2) together. By recursion on
(s-\bar{s})
, assume
(2) holds with
\mathbf{t}
and
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
replaced by their attendants

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
if
\bar{s}<s
.

For (1),
form the set
\mathbf{S}
of all triples
\bigl(T/S,\,\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\},\,\mathscr{C}\bigr)
with
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
of
T
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
, and
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}

recursively compressed for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
if
\bar{s}<s
. Let’s see
\mathbf{S}

is nonempty.

Let
K
be a residue field of
S
, and set
T:=\mathop{\rm Spec}(K)
. Then
T
is
reduced and irreducible. So (
7.8
) yields a recursively maximal
set
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
for
\mathbf{t}
and
T
. So (
7.6
)(3) yields a

T^{\sharp}/T
and a
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\sharp}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
of
T^{\sharp}
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
. If
\bar{s}=s
, then

\bigl(T^{\sharp},\,\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\},\,\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\bigr)\in\mathbf{S}
.

Suppose
\bar{s}<s
. Let
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
be the attendant to
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
. Then
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\sharp}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}
by (
4.14
)(3) with
n:=\bar{s}+1
. But

\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
is recursively maximal for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
T^{\sharp}
by
(
7.6
)(1) and (
4.14
)(1). Apply (2) with
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and

\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
for
\mathbf{t}
and
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
; thus
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
is recursively compressed for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
. Hence

\bigl(T^{\sharp},\,\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\},\,\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\bigr)\in\mathbf{S}
. Thus
\mathbf{S}\neq\emptyset
for any
\bar{s}
.

For
\bigl(T/S,\,\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\},\,\mathscr{C}\bigr)\in\mathbf{S}
, the numbers
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C})
are bounded owing to (
7.1.1
). So there exists

\bigl(T/S,\,\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\},\,\mathscr{C}\bigr)\in\mathbf{S}
with
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C})

maximal. Thus (1) holds.

For (2),
first assume
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
is recursively compressed for
\mathbf{t}
.
Suppose
\bar{s}<s
. Then
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
is recursively
compressed for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
by (
7.9
)(1). Apply (2) with
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and

\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
for
\mathbf{t}
and
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
; thus
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\sharp}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}
. Hence
(
4.14
)(3) yields
\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}=\mathbf{h}_{m}
for
m>\bar{s}
.

For any
\bar{s}
, note that (
7.6
)(2) yields
\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)
for all
m
and
p
. Hence
\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{m}^{\sharp}(p)\leq\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)
for all
m
. with equality iff
\{\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
. Thus, owing to the preceding paragraph, it remains to
prove
\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{m}^{\sharp}(p)\geq\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)
for
m\leq\bar{s}
.

Note (
7.6
)(3) gives
T/S
and
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
. So
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}}
by (
4.14
)(3), and
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}

has
T
-socle type
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
by (
4.14
)(2) with
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}
for
\mathscr{C}
. So (
7.9
)(1) holds by (2) with
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}

and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}
for
\mathbf{t}
and
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
. But, by assumption,

\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
is recursively compressed for
\mathbf{t}
. Thus
(
7.9
)(2), with
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
for
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}
and
\mathscr{C}
, yields
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C})\leq\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\sharp})
.

Note
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C})=\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(p)
and
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\sharp})=\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}^{\sharp}(p)
by (
7.1.1
) as both
\mathscr{C}
and

\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
are of socle type
\mathbf{t}
. Moreover,
\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}=\mathbf{h}_{m}
and
\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}^{\sharp}=\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}
for
m\leq\bar{s}
by the
end of (
7.1
). But,
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C})\leq\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\sharp})
. Thus

\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)\leq\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}(p)
for
m\leq\bar{s}
, as
desired.

Conversely, assume
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}\}
. Then
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\sharp}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
. Two paragraphs above,
take
C
to be
C^{\sharp}
. Thus (
7.9
)(1) holds and it remains to
prove (
7.9
)(2), both for
C^{\sharp}
.

By (1), there exists a recursively compressed
\mathscr{C}^{\flat}
for
\mathbf{t}
in
some
\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\flat}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{m}\}}
. So
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\diamond})\leq\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\flat})
by (
7.9
)(2) for
\mathscr{C}^{\flat}
.
However, it’s proved above that, if
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
is recursively
compressed for
\mathbf{t}
, then
\{\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
; thus

\{\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
. So
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\flat})=\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(p)
by (
7.1.1
). However,
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}\}
by assumption; so
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\sharp})=\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}(p)
too by (
7.1.1
). Thus
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\diamond})\leq\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{C}^{\sharp})
, as desired.
∎

8.
Permissible Socle Types

(8.1) Setup.

Keep the setup of (
7.1
). Also, assume each graded piece
\mathscr{A}_{p}
is locally free, say of rank
\mathbf{a}(p)
; recall
\mathscr{A}/F^{1}\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{O}_{S}
, so
\mathbf{a}(0)=1
. Next, for all
m
and
p
, set

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)

\displaystyle:=\textstyle\sum_{q=m}^{s}\mathbf{t}(q)\,\mathbf{a}(q-p),

(8.1.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)

\displaystyle:=\min\{\,\mathbf{g}_{m}(p),\,\mathbf{b}(p)\,\},

(8.1.2)

\displaystyle\beta^{\rm I}_{m}

\displaystyle:=\textstyle\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)\beta^{\rm I}:=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}.

(8.1.3)

Call
\{\mathbf{g}_{m}\}
the
pre-
I
-set
of
\mathbf{t}
, and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
the I-
set
of
\mathbf{t}
.

Assume
\mathbf{t}(p)\geq 0
for all
p
. Then
\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)\geq 0
and
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)\geq 0
for all
m
.

Notice that the inequalities in (
8.2
) below may become
stricter when
\mathscr{A}
is replaced by
\mathscr{A}/(0:\mathscr{B})
, so that
(0:\mathscr{B})=0
, and that this replacement may be necessary if the bound is to
be achieved. Thus, from a practical standpoint, it is reasonable to
make this replacement. However, from a logical standpoint, this
replacement has no effect on the theory in this section and the
following ones.

(8.2) Proposition
(Maximality of
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}
)
.

Fix
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
of
S
-socle type

\mathbf{t}
. Then
\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)
for all
m
and
p
;
also
\mathop{\rm rank}(\Lambda^{m}\mathscr{C})\leq\beta^{\rm I}_{m}
, with equality iff
\mathbf{h}_{m}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}
.

Proof.

Note
\mathscr{C}^{*}
is of local generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
by (
4.5
).
So locally there’s a set of homogeneous generators of
\mathscr{C}^{*}
with

\mathbf{t}(q)
elements in degree
-q
. Hence locally
\mathscr{(}\Delta^{m}C^{*})_{-p}
is generated by
\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
elements. So
\mathbf{h}_{m}(p):=\mathop{\rm rank}\mathscr{(}\Delta^{m}C^{*})_{-p}\leq\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
. But
\mathscr{(}\Delta^{m}C^{*})_{-p}\subset\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{-p}
; so
\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{b}(p)
. Thus
\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)
.

Since
\mathop{\rm rank}(\Lambda^{m}\mathscr{C})=\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}_{m}(p)
, the second assertion
follows directly.
∎

(8.3)  (Permissible socle types).

Call
\mathbf{t}

permissible
if there’s
v\geq 1
with

(a)

\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<v-1
, and

(b)

\mathbf{b}(v)>\mathbf{g}_{v}(v)
, and

(c)

\mathbf{t}(v-1)=\max\,\{\,0,\ \mathbf{b}(v-1)-\mathbf{g}_{v}(v-1)\,\}
, and

(d)

\mathbf{b}(p)>\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
for
v_{m}\leq p\leq s
and
all
m
with
v_{m}:=\inf\{\,p\mid\mathbf{b}(p)>\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)\,\}
.

This definition is virtually Iarrobino’s Def. 2.2 in

[
Iar84
]
*p. 344, but here
s
replaces
j
.

Iarrobino assumed
S=\mathop{\rm Spec}k
with
k
an infinite field,
\mathscr{A}
a
polynomial ring in two or more variables over
\mathscr{O}_{S}
, and
\mathscr{B}:=\mathscr{A}
. His proof of Prop. 3.6 on p. 356 in
[
Iar84
]
and his
discussion in § 4E on p. 372 yield the existence of
\mathscr{C}
in

\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
, provided
\mathbf{t}
is
permissible. He assumed
v\geq 2
, but it’s easy to handle the case
v=1
; see the end of this subsection. The existence of
\mathscr{C}
was
reproved by Fröberg and Laksov
[
F-L83
]
*Thm. 14, p. 142; see
also Laksov
[
JPAA217
]
*Cor. 5.5, p. 2262. In the level case
(
\bar{s}=s
) Boij, in Prop. 3.5 on p. 368 in
[
BoijJA226
]
,
removed the restriction that
k
be infinite, and generalized the
existence to
\mathscr{B}:=\mathscr{A}^{\oplus n}
for
n\geq 1
.

In Iarrobino’s setup, (d) doesn’t appear explicitly, as it’s automatic.
Plainly, it holds whenever the difference
\mathbf{b}(p)-\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
is
nondecreasing in
p
. The latter holds whenever
\mathbf{a}(p)
and
\mathbf{b}(p)
are nondecreasing in
p
, because (
8.1.1
) yields

\textstyle\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)-\mathbf{g}_{m}(p+1)=\sum_{q=m}^{s}\mathbf{t}(q)\bigl(\mathbf{a}(q-p)-\mathbf{a}(q-(p+1))\bigr)\geq 0.

Notably, (a)–(d) can hold if
\mathscr{A}
or
\mathscr{B}
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-Artinian.

By (
8.4
)(1) below,
v
is unique; in fact,
v=v_{0}
.
Moreover, as Iarrobino asserted in Def. 2.2 in
[
Iar84
]
and is
proved in (
8.4
)(1) in full generality,
v
is the
initial
value
of any
\mathscr{B}_{T}/\mathscr{I}
in
\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
; that is,
\mathscr{I}_{p}=0
for
p<v
but
\mathscr{I}_{v}\neq 0
.

Suppose
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Then so is its attendant
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
if

\bar{s}<s
by (
8.4
)(3). Moreover, Iarrobino’s proof of
Prop. 3.6 on p. 356 in
[
Iar84
]
is set up to proceed by recursion
on
\bar{s}
much as in (
7.3
) and (
7.4
). Thus there
exist
\mathscr{C}
in
\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
.

Suppose
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Then
\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible by
(
8.4
)(4), and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
is fit for
\mathbf{t}
by
(
8.4
)(5). Thus all the theory developed in
(
7.1
)–(
7.10
) applies to
\mathbf{t}
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
.
In particular, by (
7.1
), for any
T/S
, every
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is of
T
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
.

In Iarrobino’s setup, a simpler formulation of permissibility was given
by Fröberg and Laksov in
[
F-L83
]
, and presented more clearly by
Zanello in Def. 2.1 on p. 183 and Prop. 2.6 on p. 184 in

[
ZanI
]
. Namely,
\mathbf{t}
is permissible iff
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<b
, where

b:=\inf\{\,p\geq 0\mid\mathbf{b}(p)\geq\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)\,\}

(8.3.1)

The equivalence of the two formulations isn’t proved in either

[
F-L83
]
or
[
ZanI
]
, but is in (
8.4
)(2) below after
b

is replaced by
b_{1}
where

b_{1}:=\begin{cases}v_{0}-1&\text{if\kern 5.0pt}\mathbf{b}(v_{0}-1)=\mathbf{g}_{v_{0}-1}(v_{0}-1),\\
v_{0}&\text{if\kern 5.0pt}\mathbf{b}(v_{0}-1)<\mathbf{g}_{v_{0}-1}(v_{0}-1).\end{cases}

(8.3.2)

In this connection, note
\mathbf{g}_{0}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
for
p\geq 0
by
(
8.1.1
); hence, (d) above yields

v_{0}:=\inf\{\,p\geq 0\mid\mathbf{b}(p)>\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)\,\}.

(8.3.3)

Consequently,
\mathbf{b}(p)\leq\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
for
p\leq v_{0}-1
.
In particular,
b_{1}
is well defined.

Plainly,
b_{1}=b
iff
\mathbf{b}(p)<\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
for
0\leq p\leq v_{0}-2
.
The latter holds, notably, in Iarrobino’s setup; here’s why. Set
\mathbf{f}(p):=\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)-\mathbf{g}_{p+1}(p+1)
. Then (
8.1.1
) yields

\textstyle\mathbf{f}(p)=\mathbf{t}(p)\mathbf{a}(0)+\sum_{q=p+1}^{s}\mathbf{t}(q)\bigl(\mathbf{a}(q-p)-\mathbf{a}(q-(p+1))\bigr).

But
\mathbf{a}(p)
is strictly increasing for
p\geq 0
. So
\mathbf{f}(p)\geq\mathbf{t}(s)\bigl(\mathbf{a}(s-p)-\mathbf{a}(s-(p+1))\bigr)>0
. However,
\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{a}
. Thus
\mathbf{b}(p)-\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
is strictly increasing for
p\geq 0
. However,
\mathbf{b}(v_{0}-1)\leq\mathbf{g}_{v_{0}-1}(v_{0}-1)
. Thus
\mathbf{b}(p)<\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
for
0\leq p\leq v_{0}-2
, as desired.

In Iarrobino’s setup, Zanello in Rmk. 2.2 on p. 183 in
[
ZanI
]

observed that, if
\mathbf{t}
is permissible, then
b\geq s/2
. Let’s see
that his proof yields
b_{1}\geq s/2
whenever
\mathbf{b}(p)\leq\mathbf{t}(s)\mathbf{a}(p)
and
\mathbf{a}(p)<\mathbf{a}(s-p)
for
p<s/2
. Indeed, suppose
b_{1}<s/2
. Now, (
8.1.1
) yields
\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}}(b_{1})\geq\mathbf{t}(s)\mathbf{a}(s-b_{1})
. So
\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}}(b_{1})>\mathbf{t}(s)\mathbf{a}(b_{1})\geq\mathbf{b}(b_{1})
.
So (
8.3.3
) implies
b_{1}\neq v_{0}
and also
b_{1}\neq v_{0}-1
if

g_{v_{0}-1}(v_{0}-1)=b(v_{0}-1)
. Hence (
8.3.2
) is contradicted.
Thus
b_{1}\geq s/2
.

Finally, in Iarrobino’s setup, suppose
\mathbf{t}
is permissible but
v=1
.
Then
v=v_{0}
by (
8.4
)(1) below, and
b_{1}\geq s/2
as shown
above. But
v_{0}\geq b_{1}
by (
8.3.2
). Thus
1\geq s/2
, so

s\leq 2
. Now,
\mathbf{b}(1)>\mathbf{g}_{1}(1)
by (b) above as
v=1
, and
\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{a}
. But
\mathbf{g}_{1}(1)=\mathbf{t}(1)+\mathbf{t}(2)\mathbf{a}(1)
by
(
8.1.1
). So
\mathbf{a}(1)>\mathbf{t}(2)\mathbf{a}(1)
. So
\mathbf{t}(2)=0
.
So
s=1
. Thus
\mathbf{t}
is level of socle degree 1. Set
t:=\mathbf{t}(1)
. Say the variables of
\mathscr{A}
are
X_{1},\dotsc,X_{r}
. Since
r=\mathbf{b}(1)>\mathbf{g}_{1}(1)=t
, the ideal

(X_{1},\dotsc,X_{t})^{2}+(X_{t+1},\dotsc,X_{r})
gives a possible
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
, as desired.

(8.4) Lemma.

(1)
Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Then
v=v_{0}\leq s+1
, and
v
is
the initial value of any
\mathscr{I}
with
\mathscr{B}_{T}/\mathscr{I}
in

\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
for any
T/S
.

(2)
Then
\mathbf{t}
is permissible iff
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<b_{1}
and
(
8.3
)
(d)
holds.

(3)
Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Then so is its attendant
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}

provided
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\neq 0
.

(4)
Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Then
\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible.

(5)
Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Then
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
is fit for
\mathbf{t}
.

(6)
If
\mathbf{t}
is permissible, then for any
T/S
, each
\smash{\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}}
is of
T
-socle type

\mathbf{t}
.

Proof.

First, recall
\mathbf{t}(p)\geq 0
for all
p
from (
8.1
). Hence
(
8.1.1
) yields

\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)-\mathbf{g}_{m+1}(p)=\mathbf{t}(m)\,\mathbf{a}(m-p)\geq 0.

(8.4.1)

for all
m
and
p
. Now, recall
\mathbf{a}(0)=1
from (
8.1
). So
taking
m:=p
in (
8.4.1
) yields

\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)-\mathbf{g}_{p+1}(p)=\mathbf{t}(p)\geq 0.

(8.4.2)

For (1),
note
v_{0}\leq v
owing to (
8.3
)(b). Further,

\mathbf{b}(p)>\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
for
v_{0}\leq p\leq s
by hypothesis. So to show

v_{0}=v\leq s+1
, let’s show
v-1\leq s
and
\mathbf{b}(v-1)\leq\mathbf{g}_{v-1}(v-1)
, because then
v-1<v_{0}
, as well as
v_{0}\leq v
.

Note
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<v-1
by (
8.3
)(a). But
\mathbf{t}(s)\neq 0
. Thus
v-1\leq s
, as desired.

Note that taking
p:=v-1
in (
8.4.2
) yields

\mathbf{b}(v-1)-\mathbf{g}_{v}(v-1)=\bigl(\mathbf{b}(v-1)-\mathbf{g}_{v-1}(v-1)\bigr)+\mathbf{t}(v-1).

(8.4.3)

But
\mathbf{t}(v-1)\geq 0
by (
8.1
). Hence (
8.3
)(c) gives

\mathbf{b}(v-1)\leq\mathbf{g}_{v-1}(v-1)
, as desired.

For use in proving (2) below, note moreover that, if
\mathbf{b}(v-1)-\mathbf{g}_{v-1}(v-1)<0
, then (
8.4.3
) and (
8.3
)(c) imply

\mathbf{t}(v-1)=0
. But
v=v_{0}
, as was just proved. Thus
(
8.3.2
) yields that
b_{1}=v_{0}
implies
\mathbf{t}(v-1)=0
.

Given
\mathscr{B}/\mathscr{I}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
,
note
(\mathscr{B}/\mathscr{I})_{p}
is locally free of rank
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p}(p)
for
all
p
by definition; see (
4.13
). But
\mathbf{b}(p)\leq\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
for
p<v
as
v=v_{0}
by the above; so
\mathbf{b}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p}(p)
for
p<v
by (
8.1.2
). Hence
(\mathscr{B}/\mathscr{I})_{p}
is
of rank
\mathbf{b}(p)
for
p<v
. Thus
\mathscr{I}_{p}=0
for
p<v
.

Finally,
\mathbf{g}_{v}(v)<\mathbf{b}(v)
by (
8.3
)(b). So
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{v}(v)<\mathbf{b}(v)
by (
8.1.2
). Hence
(\mathscr{B}/\mathscr{I})_{v}
is of
rank strictly less than
\mathbf{b}(v)
. Thus
\mathscr{I}_{v}\neq 0
. Thus (1)
holds.

For (2),
first assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Note
b_{1}\leq v_{0}

by (
8.3.2
). Recall
v_{0}=v
by (1), and its proof notes
\mathbf{t}(v-1)=0
if
b_{1}=v_{0}
. But
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<v-1
by
(
8.3
)(a). Thus
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<b_{1}
, whether
b_{1}\leq v-1
or
b_{1}=v
.

Conversely, assume
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<b_{1}
. Notice
(
8.1.1
) does not involve
v
. So set
v:=v_{0}
. Then
(
8.3.2
) yields (
8.3
)(a), (b).

For (
8.3
)(c), first assume
b_{1}=v-1
. Then
\mathbf{b}(v-1)=\mathbf{g}_{v-1}(v-1)
by (
8.3.2
). Thus (
8.4.3
) reduces
(
8.3
)(c) to the tautology
\mathbf{t}(v-1)=\mathbf{t}(v-1)
.

Finally, assume
b_{1}=v
. Then
\mathbf{b}(v-1)<\mathbf{g}_{v-1}(v-1)
by
(
8.3.2
). But
\mathbf{t}(v-1)=0
by hypothesis. Thus
(
8.4.3
) yields (
8.3
)(c). Thus (2) holds.

For (3),
from (
7.1
), recall
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(\bar{s})=0
and
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(p)=\mathbf{t}(p)
for
p\neq\bar{s}
.

Define
\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{m}
via (
8.1.1
) with
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
for
\mathbf{t}
.
However,
\mathbf{t}(p)\geq 0
for all
p
by (
8.1
). Thus
\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
for all
p
. Define
v^{\prime}_{m}
via (
8.3
)(d)
with
\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{m}
for
\mathbf{g}_{m}
. Thus
v^{\prime}_{m}\leq v_{m}
.

Define
b^{\prime}_{1}
via (
8.3.2
) with
v^{\prime}_{0}
and
\smash{\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}_{0}}}

for
v_{0}
and
\mathbf{g}_{v_{0}}
. Let’s prove
b^{\prime}_{1}\leq b_{1}
. First, if

b_{1}=v_{0}
, then
b^{\prime}_{1}\leq v^{\prime}_{0}\leq v_{0}=b_{1}
. Second, if
b_{1}=v_{0}-1
and
b^{\prime}_{1}=v^{\prime}_{0}-1
, then
b^{\prime}_{1}\leq v^{\prime}_{0}\leq v_{0}=b_{1}
. Third,
assume
b_{1}=v_{0}-1
and
b^{\prime}_{1}=v^{\prime}_{0}
. Then (
8.3.2
) yields
\mathbf{b}(v_{0}-1)=\mathbf{g}_{v_{0}-1}(v_{0}-1)
and
\mathbf{b}(v^{\prime}_{0}-1)<\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}_{0}-1}(v^{\prime}_{0}-1)
. But
\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{m}(p)\leq\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
for all
p
. Hence

v^{\prime}_{0}\neq v_{0}
. But
v^{\prime}_{0}\leq v_{0}
. So
b^{\prime}_{1}=v^{\prime}_{0}\leq v_{0}-1=b_{1}
.
Thus always
b^{\prime}_{1}\leq b_{1}
.

However,
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(p)=0
for
p<b_{1}
by (2). Thus
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(p)=0
for

p<b_{1}^{\prime}
.

Finally, (
8.3
)(d) holds for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
as plainly
g^{\prime}_{m}=g_{m}

for
m>\bar{s}
and
g^{\prime}_{m}=g_{\bar{s}+1}
for
m\leq\bar{s}
. Thus (3)
holds.

For (4),
note
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<b_{1}
by (2). But
\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})\neq 0
. So
\bar{s}\geq b_{1}
. If
\bar{s}=b_{1}
and
b_{1}=v_{0}-1
, then
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(\bar{s})
by (
8.3.2
).
Otherwise,
\bar{s}\geq v_{0}
. But
\bar{s}\leq s
. So then
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})>\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(\bar{s})
by (
8.3.3
). In either case,
(
8.4.2
) gives
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\geq\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})
.

If
\bar{s}=s
, then
\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})=0
by (
8.1.1
). Thus

\mathbf{b}(s)\geq\mathbf{t}(s)
, as required by (
7.7
).

Suppose
\bar{s}<s
. Then
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
is permissible by (3). So by
recursion, assume (
7.7
)(1).

Given any
T^{\flat}/S
and any
\{\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{m}\}
recursively maximal
for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and
T^{\flat}
with
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
the attendant to
\mathbf{t}
, note
(
7.6
)(3) gives
T^{\sharp}/T^{\flat}
and
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\sharp}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{m}\}}
of
T^{\sharp}
-socle type

\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
.

Define
\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{m}
via (
8.1.1
) with
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
for
\mathbf{t}
. So

\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\leq\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})
by
(
8.2
) and (
8.1.2
). But
\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})
by (
8.1.1
), as
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(\bar{s})=0

and
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(p)=\mathbf{t}(p)
for
p\neq\bar{s}
by (
7.1
). Hence

\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\geq\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})
. But
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\geq\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})
as noted above. Thus
\mathbf{b}(\bar{s})-\mathbf{h}^{\flat}_{\bar{s}+1}(\bar{s})\geq\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})
, as required by
(
7.7
)(2). Thus
\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible.

For (5),
recall
\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)\geq\mathbf{g}_{m+1}(p)
from
(
8.4.1
). Recall
\mathbf{g}_{m+1}(p)\geq 0
from (
8.1
).
Thus (
8.1.2
) gives
\mathbf{b}(p)\geq\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)\geq\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m+1}(p)\geq 0
; that is, (
7.1
)(2) holds.

Next, note (
8.1.1
) gives
\mathbf{g}_{m}=0
for
m>s
. So
(
8.1.2
) gives
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}=0
for
m>s
; that is,
(
7.1
)(3) holds.

It remains to show (
7.1
)(1), or
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p+1}(p)=\mathbf{t}(p)
for all
p
.

First, suppose
p\geq b_{1}
. Let’s prove
\mathbf{b}(p)\geq\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
. If

p=b_{1}=v_{0}-1
, then
\mathbf{b}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
by (
8.3.2
).
Otherwise,
p\geq v_{0}
. If also
p\leq s
, then
\mathbf{b}(p)>\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)

by hypothesis. Finally, if
p>s
, then
\mathbf{g}_{p}=0
by
(
8.1.1
); so again
\mathbf{b}(p)\geq\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
.

Recall
\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)-\mathbf{g}_{p+1}(p)=\mathbf{t}(p)\geq 0
from
(
8.4.2
). So
\mathbf{b}(p)\geq\mathbf{g}_{p+1}(p)
too. So
(
8.1.2
) gives
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
and
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p+1}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{p+1}(p)
. Thus
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p+1}(p)=\mathbf{t}(p)
.

Finally, suppose
p<b_{1}
. Then
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
by (2). So
\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{p+1}(p)
by (
8.4.2
). But
p<b_{1}\leq v_{0}
by
(
8.3.2
); hence, (
8.3.1
) yields
\mathbf{b}(p)\leq\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
. Hence (
8.1.2
) gives
\mathbf{b}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p+1}(p)
. Thus
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{p+1}(p)=0=\mathbf{t}(p)
. Thus (5) holds.

Notice (6)
results from (5) and (
7.1
).
∎

(8.5) Lemma.

(1)
Assume (
8.3
)
(d)
. Then
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)
for
p\geq b_{1}
.

(2)
Always
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
for
p<b_{1}
.

Proof.

In (1),
if
p=b_{1}=v_{0}-1
, then
\mathbf{b}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
by
(
8.3.2
). Otherwise,
p\geq v_{0}
. If
v_{0}\leq p\leq s
, then

\mathbf{b}(p)>\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
by (
8.3
)(d) and (
8.3.3
). If
p>s
, then
g_{p}=0
by (
8.1.1
); so
\mathbf{b}(p)\geq\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
. But
\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)
owing to
(
8.1.1
). Thus in any case,
\mathbf{b}(p)\geq\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)
.
Thus (
8.1.2
) yields
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)
.

For (2),
note
p\leq v_{0}
by (
8.3.2
). So
\mathbf{b}(p)\leq\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)
by (
8.3.3
). But again,
\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)

owing to (
8.1.1
). Thus (
8.1.2
) yields
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
.
∎

(8.6) Proposition.

Assume
\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}\neq\emptyset

for some
T/S
, and
\mathscr{A}_{1}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{-p}=\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{1-p}
for
p\leq b_{1}
. Then
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<b_{1}
, and if (
8.3
)
(d)
holds,
then
\mathbf{t}
is permissible.

Proof.

Note replacing
T
by
S
, we may assume
T=S
. Fix
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
.

Given
p\leq b_{1}
with
\mathscr{B}_{p}=\mathscr{C}_{p}
, let’s see
\mathscr{B}_{p-1}=\mathscr{C}_{p-1}
and
\mathbf{t}(p-1)=0
. Note

\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{1-p}=\mathscr{A}_{1}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{-p}=\mathscr{A}_{1}\mathscr{C}^{*}_{-p}\subset\mathscr{C}^{*}_{1-p}\subset\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{1-p}.

So
\mathscr{A}_{1}\mathscr{C}^{*}_{-p}=\mathscr{C}^{*}_{1-p}=\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{1-p}
. Thus
\mathscr{B}_{p-1}=\mathscr{C}_{p-1}
.

Note
\mathscr{A}_{1}\mathscr{C}^{*}_{-p}\subset(F^{1}\mathscr{A}C^{*})_{1-p}\subset\mathscr{C}^{*}_{1-p}
. So
(F^{1}\mathscr{A}\cdot\mathscr{C}^{*})_{1-p}=\mathscr{C}^{*}_{1-p}
as
\mathscr{A}_{1}\mathscr{C}^{*}_{-p}=\mathscr{C}^{*}_{1-p}
. But
\mathbf{t}^{*}
is the Hilbert function
of
\mathscr{C}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S}
; that is,

\mathbf{t}^{*}(q)=\mathop{\rm rank}\big(\mathscr{C}^{*}_{q}\big/(F^{1}\mathscr{A}\cdot\mathscr{C}^{*})_{q}\big)\text{\quad for all }q.

(8.6.1)

So
\mathbf{t}^{*}(1-p)=0
. Thus
\mathbf{t}(p-1)=0
.

Note
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
by
hypothesis, and
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
for
p<b_{1}
by
(
8.5
)(2). So
\mathscr{B}_{p}=\mathscr{C}_{p}
for
p<b_{1}
. Thus, by the
above,
\mathbf{t}(p-1)=0
for
p<b_{1}
.

It remains to prove
\mathbf{t}(b_{1}-1)=0
. There are two cases. First, if

b_{1}=v_{0}-1
, then (
8.3.2
) gives
\mathbf{b}(b_{1})=\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}}(b_{1})
. But
\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}}(b_{1})=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(b_{1})
owing to
(
8.1.1
). Hence
\mathscr{B}_{b_{1}}=\mathscr{C}_{b_{1}}
.
Thus, by the above,
\mathbf{t}(b_{1}-1)=0
.

Second, suppose
b_{1}=v_{0}
. Then (
8.3.2
) gives
\mathbf{b}(b_{1}-1)<\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}-1}(b_{1}-1)
.

Proceeding by contradiction, suppose also
\mathbf{t}(b_{1}-1)\neq 0
. Then
(
8.6.1
) gives
(F^{1}\mathscr{A}\cdot\mathscr{C}^{*})_{1-b_{1}}\subsetneq\mathscr{C}^{*}_{1-b_{1}}
. But
\mathscr{C}^{*}_{1-b_{1}}\subset\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{1-b_{1}}
. Thus

\mathop{\rm rank}(F^{1}\mathscr{A}\cdot\mathscr{C}^{*})_{1-b_{1}}<\mathbf{b}(b_{1}-1)
.

Note
(F^{1}\mathscr{A}\cdot\mathscr{C}^{*})_{1-b_{1}}=(\Delta^{b_{1}}C^{*})_{1-b_{1}}
owing
to (
4.13.1
). But
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
; so
\mathop{\rm rank}(\Delta^{b_{1}}C^{*})_{1-b_{1}}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{b_{1}}(b_{1}-1)
. Therefore,
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{b_{1}}(b_{1}-1)<\mathbf{b}(b_{1}-1)
. So (
8.1.2
) gives
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{b_{1}}(b_{1}-1)=\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}}(b_{1}-1)
. Thus
\mathop{\rm rank}(F^{1}\mathscr{A}\cdot\mathscr{C}^{*})_{1-b_{1}}=\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}}(b_{1}-1)
.

Hence (
8.6.1
) gives
\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}}(b_{1}-1)+\mathbf{t}(b_{1}-1)=\mathop{\rm rank}\mathscr{C}^{*}_{1-b_{1}}
. But (
8.4.2
) gives
\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}}(b_{1}-1)+\mathbf{t}(b_{1}-1)=\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}-1}(b_{1}-1)
. So
\mathbf{g}_{b_{1}-1}(b_{1}-1)=\mathop{\rm rank}\mathscr{C}^{*}_{1-b_{1}}\leq\mathbf{b}(b_{1}-1)
, a contradiction. Thus
\mathbf{t}(b_{1}-1)=0
, as desired.

Finally, if (
8.3
)(d) holds, then (
8.4
)(2) now implies
\mathbf{t}
is permissible.
∎

(8.7) Proposition.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Fix
T/S
and
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
. Then
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
is recursively maximal for
\mathbf{t}
and
S
, and
\mathscr{C}
is
recursively compressed for
\mathbf{t}
.

Proof.

Consider (
7.6
)(1). Assume
\bar{s}<s
. Let
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
and

\{(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m})^{\prime}\}
be the attendants to
\mathbf{t}
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
Set
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}:=\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}
. Then
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m})^{\prime}\}}
by (
4.14
)(3).
Note that
(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p))^{\prime}=\min\{\,\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{m}(p),\,\mathbf{b}(p)\,\}

where
\mathbf{g}^{\prime}_{m}(p):=\textstyle\sum_{q=m}^{s}\mathbf{t}^{\prime}(q)\,\mathbf{a}(q-p)
. So

\{(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m})^{\prime}\}
is is the I-set of
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
. Moreover,
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
is permissible by (
8.4
)(3). So by recursion,
\{(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m})^{\prime}\}
is recursively maximal for
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
; that is,
(
7.6
)(1) holds.

As to (
7.6
)(2), it holds by (
8.2
) applied to
\mathscr{C}^{\diamond}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\diamond}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\diamond}_{m}\}}
.
