By hypothesis, (
7.6
)(3) holds, as
\mathbf{t}
is the
T
-socle
type of
\mathscr{C}
by (
8.4
)(6). Thus
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
is
recursively maximal for
\mathbf{t}
and
S
by (
7.6
). Finally,

\mathbf{t}
is quasi-permissible by (
8.4
)(4); so (
7.10
) now
yields that
\mathbf{C}
is recursively compressed for
\mathbf{t}
.
∎

(8.8) Corollary.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible, and
\mathscr{A}
is the symmetric algebra on
a locally free
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module. Assume
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}
, or in the
level case (namely, if
\bar{s}=s
), assume
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}^{\oplus n}

for some
n\geq 1
. Then the
I
-set
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
for
\mathbf{t}
is recursively maximal for
\mathbf{t}
and
S
, and there exist a
T/S

and a
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
that’s
recursively compressed for
\mathbf{t}
. Moreover, let
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\sharp}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}\}}
be of
T^{\sharp}
-socle
type
\mathbf{t}
for any
T^{\sharp}/S
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}\}
; then
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}=\{\mathbf{h}^{\sharp}_{m}\}
iff
\mathscr{C}^{\sharp}
is recursively compressed
for
\mathbf{t}
.

Proof.

Pick a scheme point of
S
and an infinite field
K
containing its
residue field. Set
T:=\mathop{\rm Spec}(K)
. Then, as recalled in
(
8.3
), Iarrobino’s work yields recursively compressed quotients
of
\mathscr{A}_{T}
for
\mathbf{t}
, and in the level case, Boij’s work yields such
quotients of
\mathscr{A}_{T}^{\oplus n}
for any
n\geq 1
. Thus
(
8.7
) yields the first assertion.

Moreover, the second assertion now holds by (
7.10
)(2).
∎

(8.9) Proposition.

Fix
\mathbf{h}
and
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
.

(1)
Set
t:=\mathbf{t}(s)
. If
\mathbf{t}
is permissible and if
\mathbf{b}(p)\leq t\mathbf{a}(p)
and
\mathbf{a}(p)\leq\mathbf{a}(s-p)
for
p\leq s/2
, then
v>s/2
and
\mathbf{h}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
for
p\leq s/2
.

(2)
If
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}
, if
\mathscr{C}
is
S
-Gorenstein, and if
\mathbf{h}(p)=\mathbf{a}(p)
for
p\leq s/2
, then
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
and

\mathbf{a}(p)\leq\mathbf{a}(s-p)
for
p\leq s/2
.

(3)
Assume
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{C}^{*}=\mathscr{A}f
with
f\in\Gamma(\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}_{-s})
. Define
\varphi\colon\mathscr{A}(s)\to\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}
locally
by
\varphi(a):=af
. Fix
p\leq s/2
. Then the following three
conditions are equivalent:

{\rm(a)}\quad\mathbf{h}(p)=\mathbf{a}(p);\qquad{\rm(b)}\quad\varphi_{-p}\text{ is surjective;}\qquad{\rm(c)}\quad\varphi_{p-s}\text{ is injective.}

Proof.

For (1),
assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Then
\mathbf{h}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
for

p\leq s/2
iff
v>s/2
owing to (
8.4
)(1). So to prove
v>s/2
, suppose
v\leq s/2
. Now,
g_{v}(v)\geq t\mathbf{a}(s-v)
by
(
8.1.1
). Assume
\mathbf{b}(p)\leq t\mathbf{a}(p)
and
\mathbf{a}(p)\leq\mathbf{a}(s-p)
for
p\leq s/2
. It follows that
g_{v}(v)\geq\mathbf{b}(v)
,
contradicting (
8.3
)(b). Thus
v>s/2
. Thus (1) holds.

For (2),
assume
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{C}
is Gorenstein. Then

\mathbf{t}(s)=1
and
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p\neq s
. Fix any
p
. Then

\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{a}(s-p)
by (
8.1.1
). So
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\min\{\mathbf{a}(s-p),\,\mathbf{a}(p)\}
and
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(s-p)=\min\{\mathbf{a}(p),\,\mathbf{a}(s-p)\}
by (
8.1.2
). Thus

\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(s-p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
.

Assume
p\leq s/2
and
\mathbf{h}(p)=\mathbf{a}(p)
. Now,
\mathscr{C}
is Gorenstein;
so
\mathbf{h}(s-p)=\mathbf{h}(p)
by (
5.3
)(B). And
\mathscr{C}
is a
quotient of
\mathscr{A}
; so
\mathbf{h}(s-p)\leq\mathbf{a}(s-p)
. Thus
\mathbf{a}(p)\leq\mathbf{a}(s-p)
, as desired. So
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\min\{\mathbf{a}(s-p),\,\mathbf{a}(p)\}=\mathbf{a}(p)
. Thus
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{h}(p)
.

If
p\geq s/2
, then
s-p\leq s/2
, and so
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(s-p)=\mathbf{h}(s-p)=\mathbf{h}(p)
. Thus
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
, as desired.

For (3),
notice that
\varphi
factors as follows:
\varphi\colon\mathscr{A}(s)\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{C}^{*}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}
. Thus (b) holds iff
(\mathscr{C}^{*})_{-p}=(\mathscr{A}^{*})_{-p}
, hence, iff (a) holds, as
\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}/\mathscr{C}^{*}
is flat.
Finally, (c) holds iff
\mathscr{A}(s)_{p-s}=(\mathscr{C}^{*})_{p-s}
, iff
\mathbf{a}(p)=\mathbf{h}(s-p)
. But
\mathscr{C}
is Gorenstein as
\mathscr{C}^{*}
is an invertible

\mathscr{C}
-module; so
\mathbf{h}(s-p)=\mathbf{h}(p)
by (
5.3
)(B). Thus
(c) holds iff (a) does.
∎

(8.10) Corollary.

Let
k
be a field,
R:=k[X_{1},\dotsc,X_{r}]
the polynomial ring. Set

\mathbf{r}(p):=\binom{p+r-1}{r-1}=\dim R_{p}
. Fix integers
a,\ q,\ s

with
1\leq s\leq a
and
1\leq q\leq\mathbf{r}(\lceil\frac{s}{2}\rceil)
. Fix a
nonzero
g\in R_{a-s}
. Fix
a_{ij}\in k
for
1\leq i\leq q
and
1\leq j\leq r
. For
p\geq 0
, set

\displaystyle L_{i}^{[p]}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{p_{j}\geq 0\text{ and }\sum p_{j}=p}a_{i1}^{p_{1}}\dotsb a_{ir}^{p_{r}}X_{1}^{-p_{1}}\dotsb X_{r}^{-p_{r}}\quad\text{for }1\leq i\leq q,

(8.10.1)

\displaystyle V_{p}

\displaystyle:=\bigl\{\,h\in R_{p}\bigm|h(a_{i1},\dotsc,a_{ir})=0\text{ for }1\leq i\leq q\,\bigr\}.

(8.10.2)

Assume the
a_{ij}
are general, meaning:
(a)

\dim V_{p}=\max\{\,\mathbf{r}(p)-q,\,0\,\}
for
p\leq s/2
and
a-s/2\leq p\leq a
, and
(b)

g(a_{i1},\dotsc,a_{ir})\neq 0
for all
i
. Fix nonzero
a_{i}\in k

for
1\leq i\leq q
. Set
f:=\sum_{i=1}^{q}a_{i}L_{i}^{[a]}
. Set
A:=(Rf)^{*}
and
C:=(Rgf)^{*}
; denote their Hilbert functions by
\bf a

and
\bf h
. Then
\mathbf{a}(p)=\mathbf{h}(p)=\min\{\,q,\,\mathbf{r}(p)\,\}
for

p\leq s/2
, and
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}^{\rm I}
where
\mathbf{t}(p)=0

for
p\neq s
and
\mathbf{t}(p)=1
for
p=s
and so
\bar{s}=s
.

Proof.

The construction here is distilled and adapted from

[
LNM1721
]
*pp. 9–14, where the
L_{i}^{[p]}
are known as
power sums
.

Note
X_{j}L_{i}^{[p]}=a_{ij}L_{i}^{[p-1]}
for
p\geq 1
. So induction on

w\leq p
yields the following key formula
(cf.
[
LNM1721
]
*(1.1.10), p. 9), provided
h
is a monomial:

hL_{i}^{[p]}=h(a_{i1},\dotsc,a_{ir})L_{i}^{[p-w]}\text{\quad for any }h\in R_{w}\text{ and any }i.

(8.10.3)

Hence, linearity in
h
yields (
8.10.3
) for any
h\in R_{w}
with

1\leq w\leq p
.

For all
p\geq 0
, set
M_{p}:=\sum_{i=1}^{q}kL_{i}^{[p]}
. Then

(0:_{R_{p}}M_{p})=V_{p}
owing to (
8.10.3
) with
w:=p

(cf.
[
LNM1721
]
*Lem. 1.15ii, p. 12). But the pairing
R_{p}\times R^{\dagger}_{p}\to k
is perfect. So (a) yields
\dim M_{p}=\min\{\,q,\,\mathbf{r}(p)\,\}
if
p\leq s/2
or if
a-s/2\leq p\leq a
. Thus if also
q\leq\mathbf{r}(p)
, then the
L_{i}^{[p]}
are linearly independent
(cf.
[
LNM1721
]
*Lem. 1.15iii, p. 12).

Note
V_{p}\subset(0:_{R_{p}}f)
for all
p
owing to (
8.10.3
).
Conversely, assume
p\leq s/2
and
q\leq\mathbf{r}(a-p)
. Given
h\in R_{p}

with
hf=0
, note
\sum_{i}a_{i}h(a_{i1},\dotsc,a_{ir})L_{i}^{[a-p]}=0

owing to (
8.10.3
). But the
L_{i}^{[a-p]}
are linearly independent
by the above with
a-p
for
p
; also,
a_{i}\neq 0
by hypotheses.
Therefore,
h(a_{i1},\dotsc,a_{ir})=0
for all
i
. Thus
h\in V_{p}
.

By hypothesis,
1\leq q\leq\mathbf{r}(\lceil\frac{s}{2}\rceil)
. But
\lceil\frac{s}{2}\rceil\leq a-\lfloor\frac{s}{2}\rfloor\leq a-p
, and
\mathbf{r}

is increasing. Hence
q\leq\mathbf{r}(a-p)
. Thus the above yields
V_{p}=(0:_{R_{p}}f)
for
p\leq s/2
(cf.
[
LNM1721
]
*Lem. 1.15iv,
p. 12).

Assume
p\leq s/2
. Note
R_{p}\big/(0:_{R_{p}}f)=A_{p}
. So by the
above, (a) gives
\mathbf{a}(p)=q
. Thus
\mathbf{a}(p)=\min\{\,q,\,\mathbf{r}(p)\,\}
, as desired (cf.
[
LNM1721
]
*Lem. 1.17, p. 13).

Set
b_{i}:=a_{i}g(a_{i1},\dotsc,a_{ir})
for all
i
. Note that
b_{i}\neq 0
by (b) as
a_{i}\neq 0
. Now,
gf=\sum_{i}b_{i}L_{i}^{[s]}
owing to
(
8.10.3
). Hence, with
gf
for
f
, the argument above yields
\mathbf{h}(p)=\min\{\,q,\,\mathbf{r}(p)\,\}
for
p\leq s/2
, as well. Thus
\mathbf{h}(p)=\mathbf{a}(p)
for
p\leq s/2
. Note
C
is
k
-Gorenstein of
k
-socle
type
s
. Thus (
8.9
)(2) yields
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}^{\rm I}
, as desired.
∎

(8.11) Corollary.

Fix positive integers
a,\ r,\ s
with
s\leq a
. Let
\mathscr{R}
be the
symmetric algebra on a locally free
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module of rank
r
; set

\mathbf{r}(p):=\binom{p+r-1}{r-1}
. Set
\mathbb{P}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{R}_{a})
. Fix
a
g\in\Gamma(\mathscr{R}_{a-s})
, and form the map
\gamma\colon\mathscr{R}_{s}\to\mathscr{R}_{a}
of multiplication by
g
; assume that
\gamma
is injective
and that
\mathop{\rm Cok}\gamma
is locally free. Define
\mathbf{a}
and
\mathbf{h}
by

\displaystyle\mathbf{a}(p)

\displaystyle:=\min\{\,\mathbf{r}(p),\,\mathbf{r}(a-p)\,\}\quad and

(8.11.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{h}(p)

\displaystyle:=\min\{\,\mathbf{r}(p),\,\mathbf{r}(s-p)\,\}\quad\text{for all
}p.

Then there’s an open subscheme
U
of
\mathbb{P}
such that
(a)

U
has a

K
-point for every infinite field
K
containing a residue field of

S
, and
(b)
given
T/S
, the
T
-points of
U
represent the

T
-Artinian,
T
-Gorenstein quotients
\mathscr{A}
of
\mathscr{R}_{T}
for which

\mathscr{C}:=(g\mathscr{A}^{*})^{*}
is such a quotient of
\mathscr{A}
, and the Hilbert
functions of
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{C}
are
\mathbf{a}
and
\mathbf{h}
. Moreover,

\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}^{\rm I}
where
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p\neq s
and

\mathbf{t}(p)=1
for
p=s
and so
\bar{s}=s
.

Proof.

Set
\Lambda:=\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathbf{a}}
. Recall
\Lambda
is the
scheme parameterizing the level quotients of
\mathscr{R}
with Hilbert
function
\mathbf{a}
; notice that these are the Gorenstein quotients of
socle degree
a
. Note that (
6.8
) implies
\Lambda
is a
subscheme of
\mathbb{P}
. So
\Lambda
is a closed subscheme of some open
subscheme
V
of
\mathbb{P}
. Let’s prove
\Lambda=V
. Given
\lambda\in\Lambda
, set
R:=\mathop{\rm Spec}\mathscr{O}_{\Lambda,\lambda}
and
T:=\mathop{\rm Spec}\mathscr{O}_{V,\lambda}
. It suffices to prove
T=R
.

Let
f\in(\mathscr{R}^{\dagger}_{-a})_{T}
represent the inclusion
T\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}
.
For all
p
, form the map
\mu_{p}\colon(\mathscr{R}_{p})_{T}\to(\mathscr{R}^{\dagger}_{p-a})_{T}

of multiplication by
f
; cf. (
6.8
). As
R\subset\Lambda
and
as
\mathbf{a}
is defined by (
8.11.1
), it follows that
(\mu_{p})_{R}
is
surjective for
p\geq a/2
and that it’s injective with free cokernel for

p\leq a/2
. Hence, so is
\mu_{p}
by Nakayama’s lemma and by the local
criterion of flatness. Hence
T\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}
factors through
R
. Thus

T=R
. Thus
\Lambda
is an open subscheme of
\mathbb{P}
.

Similarly, set
\Lambda^{\prime}:=\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{R}}^{\mathbf{h}}
; then
\Lambda^{\prime}
is
an open subscheme of
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{R}_{s})
. Fix any field
K
containing a
residue field of
S
. Then by virtue of the work of Iarrobino if
K
is
infinite and of Boij if
K
is finite — see (
8.3
) — both
\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\Lambda
have
K
-points; also, if
K
is infinite,
then (
8.10
) with
s=a
and
q=\mathbf{r}(\lfloor\frac{s}{2}\rfloor)
provides such points.

Set
\mathbb{V}:=\mathbb{V}(\mathop{\rm Cok}\gamma)
and
W:=\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{V}
. By
hypothesis,
\gamma
is injective, and
\mathop{\rm Cok}\gamma
is locally free. Thus

\mathbb{V}
is a vector bundle (of fiber dimension
\mathbf{r}(a)-\mathbf{r}(s)
),
and
\gamma
defines a central projection
\pi\colon W\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{R}_{s})
.

Set
U:=\Lambda\cap\pi^{-1}\Lambda^{\prime}
. Both
\Lambda_{K}
and

\pi^{-1}_{K}\Lambda^{\prime}_{K}
are nonempty open subschemes of
\mathbb{P}_{K}
; so

U_{K}
is one too. But, over an infinite base field, every nonempty
subscheme of a projective space has a rational point. So if
K
is
infinite, then
U_{K}
has a
K
-point. Thus
U
satisfies (a).
Furthermore, by construction,
U
satisfies (b).

Finally, (
8.9
)(2) immediately yields
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}^{\rm I}
, as desired.
∎

(8.12) Example.

Often, (
8.9
) gives a simple way
to see if, given an
S
-Gorenstein
\mathscr{C}
in
\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
for some
\mathbf{h}
, necessarily
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. Here are some examples based on the literature; in all cases,
A

is a quotient of a polynomial ring in
r
variables over an
algebraically closed field
k
, and unless otherwise said,
\mathop{\rm char}(k)=0

or
\mathop{\rm char}(k)>s
. Furthermore,
C
is a general Gorenstein quotient of

A
.

First, Iarrobino and Kanev
[
LNM1721
]
*Thm. 4.16, p. 111 take
A

to be a complete intersection that’s either general or monomial, and
take
s\leq d-r
where
d
is the product of the degrees of the forms
defining
A
. By deforming
A
and
C
so that the forms become
monomials, they conclude separately that
C_{p}=A_{p}
for
p\leq s/2
and
that the Hilbert function of
C
is
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
; however,
owing to (
8.9
), the former implies the later in much greater
generality.

Second, Iarrobino and Kanev
[
LNM1721
]
*Lem. 6.1, p. 209 take
A

to be the homogeneous coordinate ring of a finite subscheme
Z
of the
affine
(r-1)
-space. They assume that
Z
is smooth or that
Z
is
supported at a single point
p
and the ideal of
Z
in
\mathscr{O}_{p}
is
homogeneous. They show that, if
s\geq 2\tau
where
\tau:=\inf\{\,q\mid\dim_{k}A_{q}=\mathop{\rm length}Z\,\}
, then
C_{p}=A_{p}
for
p\leq s/2
.

Cho and Iarrobino state in
[
C-IJA241
]
*Thm. 2.1, a., p. 753 and
prove in
[
CI-JA366
]
*Thm. 3.3(i), p. 68 that
Z
need only be
Gorenstein. Boij
[
BoijBLMS31
]
*Prp. 2.4, p. 12 proved that, if

Z
is smooth, then
k
may be infinite of any characteristic;
furthermore, he specified the meaning of “general.”

Again, (
8.9
) yields
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. Cho
and Iarrobino note
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
above Lem. 2.3 on
p. 753 in
[
C-IJA241
]
, but as proof, they say only that it holds
since
\mathbf{a}(p)
is nondecreasing.

9.
I-compressed Homogeneous Quotients

(9.1) Setup.

Keep the setup of (
8.1
). So
\{\mathbf{g}_{m}\}
and
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
are the pre-I-set and I-set of
\mathbf{t}
. Also,
\bar{s}:=\inf\,\{\,p\mid\mathbf{t}(p)\neq 0\,\}
and
s:=\sup\{\,p\mid\mathbf{t}(p)\neq 0\,\}
. Finally, recall that the notion of “permissible
socle type” is defined in (
8.3
).

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Given
T/S
and a multilevel quotient

\mathscr{C}
of
\mathscr{B}_{T}
, call
\mathscr{C}
I-
compressed
if
its set of Hilbert functions is
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
. If
\mathscr{C}
is homogeneous and I-compressed, then so is

\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}C
, as its set of Hilbert functions is the attendant
to
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
by (
4.14
)(3), so plainly it’s equal to
the I-set of the attendant
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
to
\mathbf{t}
.

Note, by definition, a homogeneous
\mathscr{C}
is I-compressed iff
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
Thus (
8.8
)
yields, under its hypotheses, that
\mathscr{C}
is I-compressed iff it’s
recursively compressed for
\mathbf{t}
.

(9.2) Proposition.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible,
\mathscr{A}
is a polynomial ring over
\mathscr{O}_{S}
, and
S=\mathop{\rm Spec}k
with
k
a field. Set
\mathbf{h}(p):=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p+s)
for all
p
and
\mathscr{B}^{\sharp}:=\bigoplus_{q}\mathscr{A}(q-s)^{\oplus\mathbf{t}(q)}
.

(1)

(Cf. Miri
[
MiriCA21
]
*Prp. 2.8, p. 2846)
Assume
k

infinite,
v\geq 2
, and
\mathscr{B}:=\mathscr{A}
. Set
\widetilde{\mathbf{t}}(p):=\mathbf{t}(s-p)
. Then, in
\mathbf{H\Psi}_{\mathscr{B}^{\sharp}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
, there’s
\mathscr{D}

level of type
1
and of local generator type
\widetilde{\mathbf{t}}
.

(2)

(Cf. Boij
[
BoijJA226
]
*Prp. 3.5, p. 368)
Fix
n\geq 1
.
Assume
k
arbitrary, and
\mathscr{B}:=\mathscr{A}^{\oplus n}
. Then, in

\mathbf{H\Psi}_{\mathscr{B}^{\sharp}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
, there’s
\mathscr{D}
level of type
n

with
\mathscr{D}^{*}
level of type
\mathbf{t}(s)
.

Proof.

As recalled in (
8.3
), by Iarrobino’s work in the setup of
(1), and by Boij’s work in the setup of (2), there’s

\mathscr{C}
in
\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
of
S
-socle
type
\mathbf{t}
.

Set
\mathscr{D}:=\mathscr{C}^{*}(-s)
. Then
\mathscr{D}^{*}=\mathscr{C}(s)
. So
\mathscr{D}^{*}
is
of
S
-socle type
(\widetilde{\mathbf{t}})^{*}
. In (2), thus
\mathscr{D}^{*}
is level of
type
\mathbf{t}(s)
. And in (1), thus
\mathscr{D}
is of local generator type

\widetilde{\mathbf{t}}
.

Note
\mathscr{C}
is a quotient of
\mathscr{B}
, and
\mathscr{B}:=\mathscr{A}^{\oplus n}
,
with
n=1
in (1). Hence
\mathscr{D}
is level of
S
-socle type
n
.
Now,
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
is the Hilbert function of
\mathscr{C}
by the
end of (
7.1
). So
\mathbf{h}^{*}
is the Hilbert function of
\mathscr{D}
.

Note
\mathscr{C}
is of
S
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
. So
\mathscr{D}(s)
is of local
generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
. But
k
is a field. So
\mathscr{D}(s)
has a set
of homogeneous generators with
\mathbf{t}(p)
elements in degree
-p
. Use
them to express
\mathscr{D}(s)
as a homogeneous quotient of
\mathscr{B}^{\sharp}(s)
.
Thus
\mathscr{D}\in\mathbf{H\Psi}_{\mathscr{B}^{\sharp}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
.
∎

(9.3) Theorem.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
permissible. Set
\mathbf{H}:=\sum_{p}\mathbf{t}(p)\bigl(\mathbf{b}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)\bigr)
.

(1)
Then
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is an
S
-smooth
open subscheme of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}_{\bar{s}}}
, but
possibly empty.

(2)
Then
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is covered by
open subschemes, each one isomorphic to an open subscheme of the affine
space over
S
of fiber dimension
\mathbf{H}
.

Proof.

For (1),
set
\Lambda:=\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
. To prove
\Lambda
is
S
-smooth, use the Infinitesimal
Criterion
[
EGAIV
]
*(17.14.1), p. 98. Given a local
S
-scheme

T
, a closed subscheme
R
of
T
, and an
S
-map
\rho\colon R\to\Lambda
, we must lift
\rho
to an
S
-map
\tau\colon T\to\Lambda
.

Say
\rho
corresponds to
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
. Set
\mathscr{D}:=\mathscr{C}^{*}\subset\mathscr{B}_{R}^{\dagger}
and
\mathscr{E}:=\bigoplus_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{A}(q)^{\oplus\mathbf{t}(q)}
. Since
\mathbf{t}
is
permissible,
\mathscr{C}
is of
R
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
by (
8.4
)(6).
So
\mathscr{D}
is of local generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
. But
R
is a local
scheme. So
\mathscr{D}
has a set,
\Gamma
say, of homogeneous generators
with precisely
\mathbf{t}(p)
elements in degree
-p
. Use these generators
to define an
\mathscr{A}_{R}
-map
u\colon\mathscr{E}_{R}\to\mathscr{B}_{R}^{\dagger}
of degree
0

with image
\mathscr{D}
.

For each
m
and
p
, form the induced map
u^{m}_{p}\colon(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{E}_{R})_{-p}\to(\mathscr{B}_{R}^{\dagger})_{-p}
. Notice
\mathop{\rm Im}(u^{m}_{p})=(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D})_{-p}
. But
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
; so

(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D})_{-p}
is
R
-free of rank
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)
, and is a
direct summand of
(\mathscr{B}_{R}^{\dagger})_{-p}
. Also
(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{E}_{R})_{-p}

is free of rank
\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
, and
(\mathscr{B}_{R}^{\dagger})_{-p}
is free of rank

\mathbf{b}(p)
. By hypothesis, (
8.1.2
) holds. Thus either
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
and
u^{m}_{p}
is surjective, or
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
and
u^{m}_{p}
is injective (as
(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{E}_{R})_{-p}\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\rm Im}(u^{m}_{p})
is a surjection between free modules of the same rank) and

\mathop{\rm Cok}u^{m}_{p}
is
R
-free.

As
R
is closed in the local scheme
T
, the elements of
\Gamma
lift
to homogeneous sections of
\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
. Use them to define an
\mathscr{A}_{T}
-map
v\colon\mathscr{E}_{T}\to\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
. For each
m
and
p
, form the
induced map
v^{m}_{p}\colon(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{E}_{T})_{-p}\to(\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})_{-p}
.
Plainly
v^{m}_{p}|R=u^{m}_{p}
. Thus, by Nakayama’s Lemma, if
u^{m}_{p}
is
surjective, so is
v^{m}_{p}
, and by the Local Criterion of Flatness for
finitely generated modules, if
u^{m}_{p}
is injective and
\mathop{\rm Cok}u^{m}_{p}
is

R
-free, then
v^{m}_{p}
is injective and
\mathop{\rm Cok}v^{m}_{p}
is
T
-free.

Set
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}:=\mathop{\rm Im}v\subset\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
. Then
(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}=\mathop{\rm Im}v^{m}_{p}
. Hence, the conclusion of the preceding paragraph implies

(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}
is
T
-free of rank
h^{\rm I}_{m}(p)
. Moreover,
\mathscr{(}B_{T}^{\dagger})_{-p}\big/(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}
is
T
-free. But
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}=\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
if
m\ll 0
. So the exact sequence

0\to\mathscr{D}^{\prime}_{-p}\big/(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}\to\mathscr{(}B_{T}^{\dagger})_{-p}\big/(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}\to\mathscr{(}B_{T}^{\dagger})_{-p}\big/\mathscr{D}^{\prime}_{-p}\to 0

shows that
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}_{-p}\big/(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}
is free. Thus

\mathscr{D}^{\prime*}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
and
\mathscr{D}^{\prime*}|R=\mathscr{C}
.

Say
\mathscr{D}^{\prime*}
corresponds to
\tau\colon T\to\Lambda
. Then
\tau|R=\rho
. Thus
\Lambda
is smooth.

Next, recall
\Lambda
is a subscheme of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
by (
7.2
). So
\Lambda
is a closed subscheme of
some open subscheme
U
of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
. Let’s
prove
\Lambda=U
. Given
\lambda\in\Lambda
, set
R:=\mathop{\rm Spec}\mathscr{O}_{\Lambda,\lambda}
and
T:=\mathop{\rm Spec}\mathscr{O}_{U,\lambda}
. It suffices to
prove
T=R
.

Let
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{H\Psi}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
correspond to the
inclusion
T\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
. It suffices to
prove
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
. Indeed,
then the inclusion
T\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
factors
through
\Lambda
. However,
T\cap\Lambda=R
. Thus
T=R
, as
desired.

Set
\mathscr{C}:=\mathscr{C}^{\prime}|R
. Then
\mathscr{C}
corresponds to the inclusion
R\hookrightarrow\Lambda
because
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
. Hence
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
.
Set
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{C}^{\prime*}
and
\mathscr{D}:=\mathscr{C}^{*}
.

As above,
\mathscr{D}
has a set
\Gamma
of homogeneous generators with
precisely
\mathbf{t}(p)
elements in degree
-p
. Note
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}|R=\mathscr{D}
.
So the elements of
\Gamma
lift to homogeneous sections of
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}\subset\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
. Use them to define an
\mathscr{A}_{T}
-map
v\colon\mathscr{E}_{T}\to\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
whose image is
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
.

For each
m
and
p
, form the induced map
v^{m}_{p}\colon(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{E}_{T})_{-p}\to(\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})_{-p}
. As above, either
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
and
v^{m}_{p}
is surjective, or
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
and

v^{m}_{p}
is injective and
\mathop{\rm Cok}v^{m}_{p}
is
T
-free. But
\mathop{\rm Im}v^{m}_{p}=(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}
. Thus
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
. Thus (1) holds.

For (2),
first note
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}=\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
and
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m})^{\prime}\}}=\mathbb{HL}_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m})^{\prime}\}}

by (
7.5
). So let’s adapt the proof of (
7.8
), and so
also that of (
7.3
); let’s use their notation. Recall
L^{\prime}:=S
if
\bar{s}=s
, and
L^{\prime}:=\mathbb{HL}^{\{(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m})^{\prime}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}

if
\bar{s}<s
. Note
L^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
as
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}\neq\emptyset
. Thus, by recursion,
L^{\prime}
is
covered by open subschemes, each one isomorphic to an open subscheme of
an affine space over
S
.

From (
7.3.1
), recall
\mathbb{HL}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}:=L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
where
\mathscr{B}^{\prime}:=\mathop{\rm Ker}(\mathscr{B}_{L^{\prime}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{L}^{\prime})

and where
\overline{\mathbf{h}}:=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}-\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}+1}
. By construction,
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
is a closed subscheme
of an open subscheme,
U
say, of
\mathbb{G}:=\mathop{\mathbb{G}\rm rass}\nolimits_{\mathbf{t}(\bar{s})}(\mathscr{B}^{\prime}_{s})
. Thus, to prove
\mathbb{HL}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}}
is
covered by open subschemes, each one isomorphic to an open subscheme of
an affine space over
S
, it suffices to prove
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}=U
assuming
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}\neq\emptyset
.

Let
\mathscr{I}
be the ideal of
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
in
\mathbb{G}
. Then

L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}=U
if
\mathscr{I}=0
when
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}\neq\emptyset
.

Note
\mathscr{I}
is
S
-flat, as
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
is
S
-flat by
(1). So
\mathscr{I}_{T}
is the ideal of
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}_{T}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
in
U_{T}

for any
T/S
with
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}_{T}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}\neq\emptyset
. Thus by
Nakayama’s Lemma, to prove
\mathscr{I}=0
, it suffices to prove
\mathscr{I}_{T}=0
when
T:=\mathop{\rm Spec}K
where
K
is any residue field of
S
with

L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}_{T}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}\neq\emptyset
.

Note
\mathop{\rm Spec}K
is reduced and irreducible. Note
\mathbf{t}
is
quasi-permissible by (
8.4
)(3). Also note
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}

is the (unique) recursively maximal set for
\mathbf{t}
and
S
as follows.
Take a point in
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
. Let
K^{\prime}

be an algebraically closure of its residue field, and set
T^{\prime}:=\mathop{\rm Spec}K^{\prime}
. The map
T^{\prime}\to S
corresponds to some
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
in

\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T^{\prime}}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
. Then
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
is of

T^{\prime}
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
by (
8.4
)(4). Thus
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
is, by (
8.7
), the recursively maximal set.

Thus (
7.8
) applies. It’s proof shows
\mathbb{G}_{T}
is reduced and
irreducible, and contains
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}_{T}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
as a nonempty open
subscheme. But
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}_{T}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
is closed in
U_{T}
, and

U_{T}
is open in
\mathbb{G}_{T}
. So
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}_{T}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}
is nonempty,
open and closed in
U_{T}
, which is reduced and irreducible, So
L_{\mathscr{B}^{\prime}_{T}}^{\overline{\mathbf{h}}}=U_{T}
. Thus
\mathscr{I}_{T}=0
, as desired.

Finally, (
7.8
) implies
\mathbb{HL}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}
is of fiber dimension
\mathbf{H}
. Thus (2) holds.
∎

(9.4) Proposition.

Given
x\in S
, let
K
be its residue field
k(x)
.
Then, given
\mathscr{C}
in
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{K}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
of

K
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
, there’s an open neighborhood
T
of
x
and a

\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
of
T
-socle
type
\mathbf{t}
with
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{K}=\mathscr{C}
.

Proof.

Proceed as in the first part of the proof of (
9.3
)(1). Set

\mathscr{D}:=\mathscr{C}^{*}\subset\mathscr{B}_{K}^{\dagger}
and
\mathscr{E}:=\bigoplus_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{A}(q)^{\oplus\mathbf{t}(q)}
. By hypothesis,
\mathscr{C}
is of
K
-socle
type
\mathbf{t}
; so
\mathscr{D}
is of generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
. But
K
is a
field. So
\mathscr{D}
has a set of homogeneous generators with precisely

\mathbf{t}(p)
elements in degree
-p
. Lift these generators to sections
of
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}
over some open subscheme
T
of
S
. Use these sections
to define an
\mathscr{A}_{T}
-map
v\colon\mathscr{E}_{T}\to\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
.

For each
m
and
p
, form the induced map
v^{m}_{p}\colon(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{E}_{T})_{-p}\to(\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})_{-p}
. Essentially as in the proof of
(
9.3
)(1), it can be shown that, after
T
is replaced by a
smaller open subscheme, either
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
and
v^{m}_{p}
is
surjective, or
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{m}(p)
and
v^{m}_{p}
is injective and

\mathop{\rm Cok}v^{m}_{p}
is
T
-free.

Set
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}:=\mathop{\rm Im}v\subset\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
. Then
(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}=\mathop{\rm Im}v^{m}_{p}
. So
(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}
is
T
-free of rank
h^{\rm I}_{m}(p)
. Also,
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}_{-p}\big/(\Delta^{m}\mathscr{D}^{\prime})_{-p}
is free.
Set
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{D}^{\prime*}
. Thus
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
and
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}_{K}=\mathscr{C}
. By construction,

\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
is of generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
; so
\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
is of
T
-socle
type
\mathbf{t}
.
∎

(9.5) Example.

Even if
\mathbf{t}
is permissible,
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
is its I-set,
S=\mathop{\rm Spec}k
with
k
any field, and
\mathscr{A}
is the sheaf on
S

associated to a polynomial ring
A
, nevertheless
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}-\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
may be nonempty, even contain
k
-points. Here’s an example.

Fix variables
X
,
Y
; set
A:=k[X,Y]
. So
\mathbf{a}(p)=p+1
. Take

s:=5
and
\bar{s}:=4
; take
\mathbf{t}(s):=1
and
\mathbf{t}(\bar{s}):=1
. Then the vectors of nonzero values of
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
and

\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{s}
are

\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{s}\,:\,(1,2,3,3,2,1)\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}\,:\,(1,2,3,4,3,1).

To see
\mathbf{t}
is permissible, let’s use (
8.4
)(2). For
p\leq 4
, note that
\mathbf{g}_{p}=\mathbf{g}_{4}
. Hence
\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)=\mathbf{a}(4-p)+\mathbf{a}(5-p)=11-2p
. So
\mathbf{a}(p)-\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)=3p-10
. Thus
b=4
.
But
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<4
. Finally, recall from (
8.3
)
that (
8.3
)(d) is automatic in the current setup. Thus
\mathbf{t}

is permissible.

As in (
3.7
), view
A^{\dagger}
as the
k
-span of the Laurent
monomials
X^{-m}Y^{-n}
. Set

F:=X^{-5}+Y^{-5}G:=X^{-2}Y^{-2}.

Set
D:=AF+AG\subset A^{\dagger}
. It’s easy to check that

D=kF+(kX^{-4}+kY^{-4}+kG)+A^{\dagger}_{-3}+A^{\dagger}_{-2}+A^{\dagger}_{-1}+k.

So
D
has Hilbert function
(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}})^{*}
. Thus
D^{*}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
. Also,
D^{*}
is of

k
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
.

Note
\Delta^{s}D=AF
. So it’s easy to check that

\textstyle\Delta^{s}D=kF+\sum_{i=1}^{4}(kX^{-i}+kY^{-i})+k.

So
\Delta^{s}D
doesn’t have
(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{s})^{*}
as Hilbert function. Thus

D^{*}\notin\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
, as desired.

In passing, note
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is smooth
of fiber dimension
1\cdot(5-3)+1\cdot(6-1)
, or 7, by
(
9.3
)(2). In fact, it contains
k
-points. For instance, set

F^{\prime}:=F+X^{-4}Y^{-1}
and
D^{\prime}:=AF^{\prime}+AG
. It’s easy to check that
the Hilbert functions of
D^{\prime}
and
AF^{\prime}
are
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
and

\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{s}
. But
\Delta^{s}D^{\prime}=AF^{\prime}
. Thus
D^{\prime*}
is in
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
, as desired.

(9.6) Remark.

Consider Prop. 3.6 with
V=0
on p. 356 of
[
Iar84
]
and its dual
formulation, Thm. IIB on p. 351. Let’s correct an error in their
assertions.

For a moment, let
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
be an arbitrary set of functions, and
let
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}^{\prime}\}
and
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}
be the attendants; see (
7.1
).
Then there’s a “forgetful” map

\pi\colon\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}\to\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}_{m}\}};

it is given on
T
-points by mapping a
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H\Lambda}_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}}
to
\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}
. Dually,
\pi
maps

\mathscr{C}^{*}
to
\Delta^{\bar{s}+1}(\mathscr{C}^{*})
; that is,
\pi
“forgets” the
local minimal generators of
\mathscr{C}^{*}
of degree
-\bar{s}
.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible, and
\{\mathbf{h}_{m}\}
is the I-set
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
. Then the proof of (
9.3
)(2) shows
\pi
is
smooth.

In addition, assume
\mathscr{A}
is the symmetric algebra on a locally free

\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module. And assume
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}
, or in the level case
(namely, if
\bar{s}=s
), assume
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}^{\oplus n}
for some
n\geq 1
. Then, applied fiberwise, (
8.8
) implies each fiber of

\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}\big/S
is nonempty. Thus
the proof of (
9.3
)(2) shows
\pi
is surjective.

Finally,
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is an open
subscheme of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
by
(
9.3
)(1) and (
6.7
); similarly,
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m})^{\prime}\}}
is an open subscheme of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}+1},\mathbf{t}^{\prime}}
. The results in
[
Iar84
]

cited above assert that
\mathscr{C}\mapsto\Lambda^{\bar{s}+1}\mathscr{C}
defines
a surjective map
\Pi\colon\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}+1},\mathbf{t}^{\prime}}
when
S

is the Spec of an infinite field
k
. This assertion is false; indeed,

\Pi
is not defined at the
k
-point representing the module
D^{*}
of
(
9.5
). However, the smooth surjection
\pi
is defined at
the
k
-point representing the module
D^{\prime*}
of (
9.5
).

Thus, to correct the error in
[
Iar84
]
*Thm.IIB, simply replace

G(E)
by
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
; that is, replace
arbitrary compressed quotients by recursively compressed quotients.

(9.7) Example.

Here’s another example where
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}-\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is nonempty.
However, this time the socle type changes.

Take
S
,
\mathscr{A}
, and
A
as in (
9.5
), but
\bar{s}:=s:=5

and
\mathbf{t}(5)=1
. Then
\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)=6-p
; so
\mathbf{g}_{p}(p)\leq\mathbf{a}(p)
for
p\geq 3
. Thus
\mathbf{t}
is permissible by (
8.4
)(2).

Let
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
be the I-set. Then the vector of nonzero
values of
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{5}
is

\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{5}\,:\,(1,2,3,3,2,1).

As in (
3.7
), view
A^{\dagger}
as the
k
-span of the Laurent
monomials
X^{-m}Y^{-n}
. Set

F:=X^{-5}+Y^{-5}G:=X^{-2}Y^{-1}.

Set
D:=AF+AG\subset A^{\dagger}
. It’s easy to check that

D=kF+(kX^{-4}+kY^{-4})+(kX^{-3}+kY^{-3}+kG)+A^{\dagger}_{-2}+A^{\dagger}_{-1}+k.

So
D
has
(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}})^{*}
as Hilbert function. So
D^{*}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
. But
D
requires
two generators. So
D^{*}
isn’t of
k
-socle type
\mathbf{t}
. Thus
(
8.4
)(6) yields
D^{*}\notin\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
, as desired.

In passing, note
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is smooth
of fiber dimension
1\cdot(6-1)
, or 5, by (
9.3
)(2). In fact,
it contains
k
-points. For instance, set
F^{\prime}:=F+X^{-4}Y^{-1}
and

D^{\prime}:=AF^{\prime}
. It’s easy to check that
D^{\prime}
has Hilbert function
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{5}
. But
\Delta^{m}D^{\prime}=D^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{5}
for
m\leq 5
. Thus
D^{\prime*}
is in
\mathbf{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{A}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
, as desired.

(9.8) Proposition.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible, and
S
is irreducible.

(1)
Assume
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
is
nonempty. Then it’s irreducible.

(2)
Assume
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is
nonempty. Then it’s an open, dense subscheme of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
.

Proof.

For (1),
for
\bar{s}\leq q\leq s
, set
P_{q}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{B}_{q})^{\times\mathbf{t}(q)}
with
P_{q}:=S
if
\mathbf{t}(q)=0
. Set
P:=\prod_{q}P_{q}
.
For
1\leq i\leq\mathbf{t}(q)
, let
\mathscr{L}_{q,i}
denote the pullback to
P
,
under the projection to the
i
th factor of
P_{q}
, of
O_{\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{B}_{q})}(1)
; so
\mathscr{L}_{q,i}^{*}\subset(\mathscr{B}_{P}^{\dagger})_{-q}
. Form the
natural multiplication map

\textstyle\mu_{p}\colon\bigoplus_{q,i}(\mathscr{A}_{P})_{p+q}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{P}}\mathscr{L}^{*}_{q,i}\to(\mathscr{B}_{P}^{\dagger})_{p}\text{\quad for\kern 5.0pt}-s\leq p\leq-\bar{s}.

(9.8.1)

Given a finite function
\mathbf{h}\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}
, denote the subscheme where

\mathop{\rm rank}(\mu_{p})=\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
by
L_{p}\subset P
; that is, an
S
-map
T\to P
factors through
L_{p}
iff
\mathop{\rm Cok}((\mu_{p})_{T})
is locally free of rank

\mathbf{b}^{*}(p)-\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
. But
\mathop{\rm Cok}((\mu_{p})_{T})=(\mathop{\rm Cok}(\mu_{p}))_{T}
by
right exactness of pullback. So by the theory of flattening
stratification,
L_{p}
exists, although it might be empty. Set

\textstyle L^{\mathbf{h}}:=\bigcap_{p=-s}^{-\bar{s}}L_{p}\subset P.

Let
x\in P
be a scheme point,
K
its residue field. Then
x\in L^{\mathbf{h}}
iff the map of
K
-vector spaces
(\mu_{p})_{K}
is of rank
\mathbf{h}^{*}(p)
for
0\geq p\geq-s
. Thus as
\mathbf{h}
varies, the
L^{\mathbf{h}}

stratify
P
; that is, set-theoretically their disjoint union

\coprod_{\mathbf{h}}L^{\mathbf{h}}
is
P
.

By hypothesis,
S
is irreducible, and each
\mathscr{B}_{q}
is locally free
of finite rank. Hence
P
is irreducible too. Its generic point lies
in
L^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}}
for some
\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}
. Set
L:=L^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}}
. Then the support of
L
is an open subset of
P
, since

L
is the intersection of an open subscheme and a closed subscheme, and
the latter’s support must be all of
P
. Thus
L
is (nonempty and)
irreducible. Also, by lower semicontinuity of the rank of
\mu_{r}
, if

L^{\mathbf{h}}\neq\emptyset
for some
\mathbf{h}
, then
\mathbf{h}(r)\leq\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}(r)
for all
r
.

Given
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}\mathbf{t}}
, let

K^{\prime}
be its residue field, and
C
the corresponding quotient of
\mathscr{B}_{K^{\prime}}
, Then
C^{*}
is of (local) generator type
\bf t^{*}
. Pick a
minimal set of generators. For
0\geq p\geq-s
, this set defines a map

\bigoplus_{q}(\mathscr{A}_{K^{\prime}})_{p+q}^{\oplus\mathbf{t}(q)}\to(\mathscr{B}_{K^{\prime}}^{\dagger})_{p}

of rank
\bigl(\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}\bigr)^{*}(p)
, and so a
K^{\prime}
-point
of
L^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
. Take
\mathbf{h}:=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}

above. Thus
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(r)\leq\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}(r)
for all

r
.

Set
\mathscr{D}:=\mathop{\rm Im}\bigl(\bigoplus_{p}(\mu_{p})_{L}\bigr)
. Fix
p
. Then
\mathop{\rm rank}\mathscr{D}_{p}=\bigl(\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}\bigr)^{*}(p)
. But (
9.8.1
) yields

\textstyle\mathop{\rm rank}\mathscr{D}_{p}\leq\mathop{\rm rank}\Bigl(\bigoplus_{q}(\mathscr{A}_{\mathbb{L}})_{p+q}\otimes(\mathscr{U}^{*}_{q})_{L}\Bigr)=\mathbf{g}^{*}_{\bar{s}}(p).

Also,
\mathop{\rm rank}\mathscr{D}_{p}\leq b^{*}(p)
. Thus
\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}(-p)\leq\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(-p)
. But
\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}(-p)\geq\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(-p)
by the above paragraph. Thus
\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. But
L:=L^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm J}}
. Thus
L:=L^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
.

Notice (
9.8.1
) yields a (homogeneous) surjection

\tau\colon\bigoplus_{q,i}\mathscr{A}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{L}}(\mathscr{L}^{*}_{q,i})_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{D}
. Apply
\bullet\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{L}}\mathscr{O}_{L}
to get the surjection
\bar{\tau}\colon\bigoplus_{q,i}(\mathscr{L}^{*}_{q,i})_{L}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{D}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{L}}\mathscr{O}_{L}
.
Fix
q<b_{1}
. Then
\mathbf{t}(q)=0
by (
8.4
)(2). So there are
no
\mathscr{L}^{*}_{q,i}
. Thus
\bigl(\mathscr{D}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{L}}\mathscr{O}_{L}\bigr)_{-q}=0
. Now, fix
q\geq b_{1}
. Then
\tau_{-q}
is an isomorphism; indeed,
its target is locally free of rank
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(q)
, whereas
its source is locally free of rank
g_{\bar{s}}(q)
; however,
g_{\bar{s}}(q)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(q)
by (
8.4
)(1).
So
\bar{\tau}_{-q}
is an isomorphism. Thus
\bigl(\mathscr{D}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{L}}\mathscr{O}_{L}\bigr)_{-q}
is locally free of rank
\mathbf{t}(q)
. Thus
\mathscr{D}
is of local generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
.

Therefore,
\mathscr{D}^{*}
defines a map
L\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
owing to the universal property of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
. This map is surjective, because,
as shown above, every
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}\mathbf{t}}
gives rise to a point of
L
, and plainly the latter maps to

x^{\prime}
. But
L
is irreducible. Thus
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
is too. Thus (1) holds.

For (2),
recall
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is
an open subscheme of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}

by (
9.3
)(1). Thus (1) yields (2).
∎

(9.9) Corollary.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible, and
S
is irreducible. Assume
\mathscr{A}

is the symmetric algebra on a locally free
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module. Finally,
assume
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}
, or in the level case (namely, if
\bar{s}=s
),
assume
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}^{\oplus n}
for some
n\geq 1
.

Then
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is a nonempty, open
subscheme of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
, which
is irreducible.

Proof.

The assertions follow immediately from (
8.8
) and
(
9.8
)(1), (2).
∎

(9.10) Remark.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible, and
S
is irreducible. Then a minor
modification of the proof of (
9.8
) yields a bit more than
stated. First, let’s see that
P
parameterizes, in a weak sense, the
quotients of
\mathscr{B}
of socle type bounded by
\mathbf{t}
.

Let
x\in P
be a point,
K
a field containing its residue field. Consider
(
9.8.1
), and set
D:=\mathop{\rm Im}\bigl(\bigoplus_{p}(\mu_{p})_{K}\bigr)
.
Then
D
is generated, possibly not minimally, by homogeneous elements
of
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{K}
of which
\mathbf{t}(q)
are of degree
-q
; that is, the
(local) generator type of
D
is bounded by
\mathbf{t}^{*}
. Thus
D^{*}
is a
quotient of
\mathscr{B}_{K}
of socle type bounded by
\mathbf{t}
.

Conversely, let
K
be a field containing a residue field of
S
, and

C
a homogeneous quotient of
\mathscr{B}_{K}
of socle type bounded by
\mathbf{t}
. Then
C^{*}
is generated, possibly not minimally, by homogeneous
elements of
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{K}
of which
\mathbf{t}(q)
are of degree
-q
. Order
them to define a map
\bigoplus_{q}(\mathscr{A}_{K})_{p+q}^{\oplus\mathbf{t}(q)}\to(\mathscr{B}_{K}^{\dagger})_{p}
, and so a
K
-point of
P
representing
C
. Thus

P
parameterizes the various
C
. Notice however that different

K
-points may represent the same
C
. Furthermore,
P
carries no
universal flat family.

Second, consider the map
L\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
. Recall that
L
is open in
P
, and it parameterizes, in
the same weak sense, the various compressed
C
of socle type
\mathbf{t}
.
Furthermore, as the map is surjective and as
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is open in
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
by (
9.8
)(2), the preimage
\Lambda
of
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is open in
L
, and
parameterizes, in the same sense, the various recursively compressed
C

of socle type
\mathbf{t}
; also,
\Lambda
is nonempty under the hypotheses
of (
9.9
).

Third, consider the locus
T\subset P
just of the
C
of socle type

\mathbf{t}
. Note forming
\mathop{\rm Im}\bigl(\bigoplus_{p}\mu_{p}\bigr)
needn’t commute
with base change, but forming
\mathscr{D}^{\mathbf{h}}:=\mathop{\rm Im}\bigl(\bigoplus_{p}(\mu_{p})_{L^{\mathbf{h}}}\bigr)
does. For
1\leq i\leq\mathbf{t}(q)
, form the flatting stratification of
(\mathscr{D}^{\mathbf{h}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{P}}\mathscr{O}_{P})_{-q}
, and denote the stratum of rank
\mathbf{t}(q)
by
T^{\mathbf{h}}_{q}
. Set
T^{\mathbf{h}}:=\bigcap_{q}T^{\mathbf{h}}_{q}
. Then
T=\amalg_{\mathbf{h}}T^{\mathbf{h}}
. Thus
T
is constructible, but possibly not locally
closed.

10.
I-Compressed Filtered Quotients

(10.1) Setup.

Keep the setup of (
9.1
). So
\{\mathbf{g}_{m}\}
and

\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
are the pre-I-set and I-set of
\mathbf{t}
. Also,

\bar{s}:=\inf\,\{\,p\mid\mathbf{t}(p)\neq 0\,\}
and

s:=\sup\{\,p\mid\mathbf{t}(p)\neq 0\,\}
. Recall that
“I-Compressed” and“permissible socle type” and
v_{0}
and
b_{1}

and
\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
are defined in (
9.1
) and
(
8.3
) and (
8.3
)(d) and (
8.3.2
) and
(
8.1.3
).

(10.2)  (Stability and integrity).

Given a graded
\mathscr{A}
-module
\mathscr{C}
, call
\mathscr{C}

n
-
stable

if
\mathscr{A}_{1}\cdot\mathscr{C}_{q-1}=\mathscr{C}_{q}
for
q>n
. Say that
\mathscr{C}

has
integrity

m
if
(0:_{\mathscr{C}_{p}}\mathscr{A}_{1})=0
for
p<m
.

For example, suppose
\mathscr{A}_{1}
generates
\mathscr{A}
over
\mathscr{O}_{S}
. Then

\mathscr{C}
has integrity
m
iff
\mathop{\rm Soc}\nolimits_{k}(\mathscr{C})\subset F^{m}\mathscr{C}
, since

(0:_{\mathscr{C}}\mathscr{A}_{1})=\mathop{\rm Soc}\nolimits_{k}(\mathscr{C})
as

\mathop{\rm Soc}\nolimits_{k}(\mathscr{C})=(0:_{\mathscr{C}}F^{1}\mathscr{A})
. So for instance, if
\mathscr{A}

is the symmetric algebra on a locally free
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module, then
plainly
\mathscr{A}(q)^{\oplus r}
has integrity
m
for all
m
, all
q
,
and all
r
; furthermore,
\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}(q)^{\oplus r}
is
m
-stable for
all
m
, all
q
, and all
r
either by direct computation or by the
following proposition.

(10.3) Proposition.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a graded
\mathscr{A}
-module,
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Assume
\mathscr{C}_{p}

for
p\leq m
and
\mathscr{A}_{1}
are locally free of finite rank. Then the
pullback
\mathscr{C}_{T}
has integrity
m
for all
T/S
iff
\mathscr{C}^{\dagger}
is

(-m)
-stable.

Proof.

Fix
p<m
. Define

\mu\colon\mathscr{C}_{p}\to\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathscr{A}_{1},\,\mathscr{C}_{p+1})
using
multiplication. Adjoint associativity gives

\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathscr{A}_{1},\,\mathscr{C}_{p+1})=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathscr{A}_{1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathscr{C}_{p+1})^{*},\,\mathscr{O}_{S})
. So dualizing
\mu
gives

\mu^{*}\colon\mathscr{A}_{1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{C}^{\dagger}_{-p-1}\to\mathscr{C}^{\dagger}_{-p}
.

By the local criterion, every pullback
\mu_{T}
is injective iff
\mu

is injective and
\mathop{\rm Cok}\mu
is flat, so iff
\mu
is locally split.
But
\mu
is locally split iff
\mu^{*}
is, iff
\mu^{*}
is surjective.
Also,
\mathop{\rm Ker}(\mu_{T})=(0:_{\mathscr{C}_{T,p}}\mathscr{A}_{T,1})
. Thus every

\mathscr{C}_{T}
has integrity
m
iff
\mathscr{C}^{\dagger}
is
(-m)
-stable.
∎

(10.4) Proposition.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Fix

\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
for some
\mathbf{h}
.

(1)
Then
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})\leq\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
, with equality if
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
.

(2)
Assume that
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
, that
\mathscr{A}
has integrity
s-b_{1}
, and that
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}
is

(1-b_{1})
-stable. Then
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
and
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
and

G_{\bullet}(\mathscr{F}^{*})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S}=G_{\bullet}(\mathscr{F}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.

Proof.

Each assertion holds if it holds on some neighborhood of each point of

S
. Now,
\mathbf{t}^{*}
is the local generator type of
\mathscr{F}^{*}
; so each point of
S
has a neighborhood on which
\mathscr{F}^{*}
has a set of generators with precisely
\mathbf{t}(q)
generators in
F^{-q}\mathscr{F}^{*}-F^{1-q}\mathscr{F}^{*}
. Thus shrinking

S
, we may assume these generators exist globally on
S
.

Set
\mathscr{E}:=\bigoplus_{q}\mathscr{A}(q)^{\oplus\mathbf{t}(q)}
. The generators
define a map
\varepsilon\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}^{*}
; it’s surjective and filtered
of degree 0. Let

\overline{\varepsilon}\colon\mathscr{E}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
be the
induced map. It is surjective. Plainly

\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{E}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E})=\sum_{p\geq b_{1}}\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)

owing to (
8.1.1
). However,

\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
for
p\geq b_{1}
by
(
8.5
)(1). Thus

\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*})\leq\sum_{p\geq b_{1}}\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
.

On the other hand,
F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}\subset F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}
, and

F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is flat. Hence

\mathop{\rm rank}(F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*})\leq\sum_{p<b_{1}}\mathbf{b}(p)
. But

\mathbf{b}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
for
p<b_{1}
by
(
8.5
)(2). Therefore,

\mathop{\rm rank}(F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*})\leq\sum_{p<b_{1}}\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
. Hence
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F}^{*})\leq\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
.
Thus
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})\leq\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
.

Lastly, note
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})=\mathop{\rm rank}(G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F})
. But

G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
since

\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
; hence,

\mathop{\rm rank}(G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F})=\sum_{p}\mathbf{h}(p)
. Thus

\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
if

\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. Thus (1) holds.

For (2),
set

\beta^{\rm I}_{1}:=\sum_{p\geq b_{1}}\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
and

\beta^{\rm I}_{2}:=\sum_{p<b_{1}}\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
. Then

\beta^{\rm I}_{1}+\beta^{\rm I}_{2}=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. And

\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*})\leq\beta^{\rm I}_{1}
and

\mathop{\rm rank}(F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*})\leq\beta^{\rm I}_{2}
; see the proof of (1).
But
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F}^{*})=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
now. Thus

\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*})=\beta^{\rm I}_{1}
and

\mathop{\rm rank}(F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*})=\beta^{\rm I}_{2}
.

Note
\mathop{\rm rank}(F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger})=\beta^{\rm I}_{2}
and

F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is flat. So

F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}=F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}
. But

F^{q}\mathscr{F}^{*}=\mathscr{F}^{*}\cap F^{q}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}
for all
q
; indeed, as

\mathscr{F}
has the induced filtration, the quotient map

\mathscr{B}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{F}
is strict, and so its dual, the inclusion

\mathscr{F}^{*}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{B}^{*}
, is strict too by (
2.3
). Thus

G_{-p}\mathscr{F}^{*}=\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{-p}
for
p<b_{1}
. So

\mathbf{h}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
for
p<b
. Recall

\mathbf{b}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
for
p<b_{1}
by
(
8.5
)(2). Thus
\mathbf{h}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
for

p<b_{1}
.

By the above,

\overline{\varepsilon}\colon\mathscr{E}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is a
surjection of locally free sheaves of the same rank. So
\overline{\varepsilon}
is an
isomorphism. So its restriction to
F^{-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}

is injective. Thus

G_{-b_{1}}\varepsilon\colon G_{-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}\to G_{-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is injective.

Proceed by induction: given
p\geq b_{1}
, assume

G_{-p}\varepsilon\colon G_{-p}\mathscr{E}\to G_{-p}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is injective. Given

\sigma\in S
and
y\in G_{-p-1}\mathscr{E}_{\sigma}
with

(G_{-p-1}\varepsilon)y=0
, take any
x\in\mathscr{A}_{1,\sigma}
. Then

(G_{-p}\varepsilon)(xy)=x((G_{-p-1}\varepsilon)y)=0
. So
xy=0
. But

\mathscr{A}
has integrity
s-b_{1}
, and
p+1>b_{1}
. So
y=0
. Thus

G_{-p}\varepsilon\colon G_{-p}\mathscr{E}\to G_{-p}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is injective for all

p\geq b_{1}
.

By (
2.6
), the functor
\mathscr{M}\mapsto G_{\bullet}\mathscr{M}
is biexact on
the filtered
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-Artinian modules
\mathscr{M}
. Thus

\overline{\varepsilon}\colon\mathscr{E}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is a
strict injection.

But
\overline{\varepsilon}
is surjective. So
\overline{\varepsilon}
is a strict isomorphism. Thus

G_{\bullet}\overline{\varepsilon}
is an isomorphism.

Hence
\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{h}(p)
for
p\geq b_{1}
. But

\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
for
p\geq b_{1}
by
(
8.5
)(1). Thus
\mathbf{h}(p)=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)
for

p\geq b_{1}
, so for all
p
owing to the conclusion above, as desired.

Note
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
if

G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is of local generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
, or
((G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S})_{-p}
is locally free of rank
\mathbf{t}(p)
for all

p
. The latter holds for
p\geq b_{1}
, as
G_{\bullet}\overline{\varepsilon}
is bijective and
as
\mathscr{E}
is plainly of local generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
. But
\mathbf{t}
is
permissible; so
\mathbf{t}(p)=0
for
p<b_{1}
by (
8.4
). Thus it
remains to show
((G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S})_{-p}=0
for
p<b_{1}
.

Recall
G_{-p}\mathscr{F}^{*}=\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{-p}
for
p<b_{1}
. But, by
hypothesis,
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}
is
(1-b_{1})
-stable; that is,
\mathscr{A}_{1}\cdot\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{-p}=\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{1-p}
for
p<b_{1}
. Thus
((G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S})_{-p}=0
for
p<b_{1}-1
.

Recall
\overline{\varepsilon}\colon\mathscr{E}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}

is injective and
\varepsilon\colon\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{F}^{*}
is surjective. Hence
\varepsilon

restricts to a surjection
F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}\twoheadrightarrow F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
by the
Snake Lemma. Thus
(G_{1-b_{1}}\varepsilon)(\mathscr{E}_{1-b_{1}})=G_{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
.

Recall
\mathscr{E}
is of local generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
and
\mathbf{t}(b_{1}-1)=0
. So
(\mathscr{E}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S})_{1-b_{1}}=0
. Hence

\bigoplus_{q>0}\mathscr{A}_{q}\cdot\mathscr{E}_{1-b_{1}-q}=G_{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}
.
Therefore,

\textstyle\bigoplus_{q>0}\mathscr{A}_{q}\cdot\big((G_{1-b_{1}-q}\varepsilon)(\mathscr{E}_{1-b_{1}-q})\big)=(G_{1-b_{1}}\varepsilon)(G_{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}).

But
(G_{1-b_{1}-q}\varepsilon)(\mathscr{E}_{1-b_{1}-q})\subset G_{1-b_{1}-q}\mathscr{F}^{*}
,
and
(G_{1-b_{1}}\varepsilon)(\mathscr{E}_{1-b_{1}})=G_{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
. Hence

\bigoplus_{q>0}\mathscr{A}_{q}\cdot G_{1-b_{1}-q}\mathscr{F}^{*}=G_{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
. So

((G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S})_{1-b_{1}}=0
. Thus
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
.

Finally, by (
4.6
) there’s a canonical surjection
G_{\bullet}(\mathscr{F}^{*})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S}\twoheadrightarrow G_{\bullet}(\mathscr{F}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.
It’s an isomorphism as both its source and target are locally free with
the same Hilbert function, namely
\mathbf{t}^{*}
. Thus (2) holds.
∎

(10.5) Proposition.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. Given
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{r}}
for some
\mathbf{h}
and
\mathbf{r}
, assume
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
. Then
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
and
\mathbf{r}=\mathbf{t}
and
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
.

Proof.

As
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{r}}
and
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
, the
definitions yield
\mathbf{h}=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
.

Note that
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
. Hence
\mathop{\rm rank}(G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F})=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
by
(
10.4
)(1). However,
\mathop{\rm rank}(G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F})=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})
.
Thus
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
.

By definition,
\mathbf{r}^{*}
is the generating function of
G_{\bullet}(\mathscr{F}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, and
\mathbf{t}^{*}
is that of
(G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S}
. But there’s a canonical surjection
G_{\bullet}(\mathscr{F}^{*})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S}\twoheadrightarrow G_{\bullet}(\mathscr{F}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S})
by (
4.6
).
Thus
\mathbf{r}(p)\leq\mathbf{t}(p)
for all
p
. Define
\alpha^{\rm I}
like

\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
in (
8.1.3
) using
\mathbf{r}
in place of

\mathbf{t}
. Thus
\alpha^{\rm I}\leq\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
, and

\alpha^{\rm I}=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
iff
\mathbf{r}=\mathbf{t}
. But

\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})\leq\alpha^{\rm I}
by (
10.4
)(1). So

\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{F})\leq\alpha^{\rm I}\leq\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. So
\alpha^{\rm I}=\beta^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. Thus
\mathbf{r}=\mathbf{t}
.
∎

(10.6) Lemma.

Fix
\mathbf{h}
and
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
. Set

\mathscr{N}:=\mathscr{C}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S}
and
\mathscr{W}:=F^{-s}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}/\mathscr{C}^{*}
. Fix an affine
S
-scheme
T
. Then given a homogeneous
\mathscr{O}_{T}
-section
\nu\colon\mathscr{N}_{T}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}
of
\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{N}_{T}
,
and one
\omega\colon\mathscr{W}_{T}\hookrightarrow F^{-s}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
of
F^{-s}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{W}
and given
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
with
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{C}_{T}
, there’s a unique
\gamma\in F^{1}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{T}}(\mathscr{N}_{T},\,\mathscr{W}_{T})
with
\mathscr{F}^{*}:=\mathscr{A}_{T}\cdot(\nu+\omega\gamma)(\mathscr{N}_{T})
.

Proof.

Note that
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is a finitely generated and flat
\mathscr{O}_{T}
-module. So, as
T
is affine, each projection
F^{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}\twoheadrightarrow G_{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}
splits over
\mathscr{O}_{T}
; say
F^{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}=\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{p}\oplus F^{p+1}\mathscr{F}^{*}
. But
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*}=\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}
. Thus
F^{p}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}=\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{p}\oplus\omega(\mathscr{W}_{T})_{p}\oplus F^{p+1}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
.

Recall from (
6.1
) that
F^{q}\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{B}
for all
q\leq 0
. Hence

F^{q}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}=0
for all
q\geq 1
. It follows that
F^{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}=\bigoplus_{q\geq p}\mathscr{F}^{\prime}_{q}
and that
F^{p}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}=F^{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}\oplus\omega(F^{p}\mathscr{W}_{T})
. Denote the corresponding projections by

\varphi_{p}\colon F^{p}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}\to F^{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}
and
\pi_{p}\colon F^{p}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}\to F^{p}\mathscr{W}_{T}
.

Recall that
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*}=\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}
. So for every
p
, there’s a
corresponding projection
F^{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}\twoheadrightarrow{(\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*})}_{p}
. Fix a
lifting
\nu^{\prime}_{p}\colon(\mathscr{N}_{T})_{p}\hookrightarrow F^{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}
of
\nu_{p}\colon({\mathscr{N}_{T}})_{p}\hookrightarrow(\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*})_{p}
. Then
\nu^{\prime}_{p}-\nu_{p}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathscr{N}_{p},\,F^{p+1}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger})
. Set
\gamma_{p}:=\pi_{p+1}(\nu^{\prime}_{p}-\nu_{p})\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(({\mathscr{N}_{T}})_{p},\,F^{p+1}\mathscr{W}_{T})

and
\gamma:=\sum\gamma_{p}\in F^{1}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathscr{N}_{T},\,\mathscr{W}_{T})
.

Note that
\varphi_{p}+\omega_{p}\pi_{p}=1_{F^{p}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}}
. Therefore,

\omega_{p}\gamma_{p}=(\nu^{\prime}_{p}-\nu_{p})-\varphi_{p+1}(\nu^{\prime}_{p}-\nu_{p})

in
\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}((\mathscr{N}_{T})_{p},\,F^{p+1}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})
. Furthermore,

\nu_{p}+\omega_{p}\gamma_{p}=\nu^{\prime}_{p}-\varphi_{p+1}(\nu^{\prime}_{p}-\nu_{p})\text{\quad in\quad}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}((\mathscr{N}_{T})_{p},\,F^{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}).

Hence
(\nu+\omega\gamma)(\mathscr{N}_{T})\subset\mathscr{F}^{*}
and
G_{\bullet}(\nu+\omega\gamma)(\mathscr{N}_{T})=\mathscr{N}_{T}
. But
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*}=\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}
and

\mathscr{A}_{T}\cdot\mathscr{N}_{T}=\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}
. Apply the last paragraph of
(
2.6
). Thus
\mathscr{A}_{T}\cdot(\nu+\omega\gamma)(\mathscr{N}_{T})=\mathscr{F}^{*}
.

Lastly, given
\gamma^{\flat}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{T}}(\mathscr{N}_{T},\,\mathscr{W}_{T})
, set

\delta:=\gamma^{\flat}-\gamma
. Note
\delta\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{T}}(\mathscr{N}_{T},\,\mathscr{W}_{T})
. So
\delta(\mathscr{N}_{T})\subset\mathscr{W}_{T}
. So

(\omega\delta)(\mathscr{N}_{T})\subset\omega(\mathscr{W}_{T})
. Now, assume
(\nu+\omega\gamma^{\flat})(\mathscr{N}_{T})\subset\mathscr{F}^{*}
. Note
(\nu+\omega\gamma^{\flat})-(\nu+\omega\gamma)=\omega\delta
. Hence

(\omega\delta)(\mathscr{N}_{T})\subset\mathscr{F}^{*}
. Thus
(\omega\delta)(\mathscr{N}_{T})\subset\mathscr{F}^{*}\cap\omega(\mathscr{W}_{T})
. But
F^{p}\mathscr{F}^{*}\cap\omega(F^{p}\mathscr{W}_{T})=0
for all
p
. So
(\omega\delta)(\mathscr{N}_{T})=0
.
So
\omega\delta=0
. But
\omega
is injective. So
\delta=0
. Thus

\gamma^{\flat}=\gamma
, as desired.
∎

(10.7) Definition.

Fix
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
for some
\mathbf{h}
. Given a
filtered quotient
\mathscr{F}
of
\mathscr{B}_{T}
for some
T/S
, call
\mathscr{F}
a

lifting
of
\mathscr{C}
if
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{C}_{T}
as quotients of

\mathscr{B}_{T}
(so
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h}}
).

Set
\mathscr{W}:=F^{-s}\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}/\mathscr{C}^{*}
and
\mathscr{N}:=\mathscr{C}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S}
. Call
\mathscr{F}
a
potential lifting
of
\mathscr{C}
if

there exist homogeneous sections
\nu\colon\mathscr{N}_{T}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}
of
\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{N}_{T}
and
\omega\colon\mathscr{W}_{T}\hookrightarrow F^{-s}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
of

F^{-s}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{W}
and a map
\gamma\in F^{1}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{T}}(\mathscr{N}_{T},\,\mathscr{W}_{T})
with
\mathscr{F}^{*}:=\mathscr{A}_{T}\cdot(\nu+\omega\gamma)(\mathscr{N}_{T})
.

Call
\mathscr{C}

effective
if any potential lifting
\mathscr{F}
on any

(Noetherian) affine
T
is, in fact, a lifting. Call
\mathscr{C}

very
effective
if, in addition,
\mathscr{F}
has the same socle type
\mathbf{t}
as

\mathscr{C}
.

(10.8) Proposition.

Every
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}

with
\mathbf{t}
permissible is very effective.

Proof.

Fix a potential lifting
\mathscr{F}
of
\mathscr{C}
, and use the setup of
(
10.7
). Set
\mathscr{E}:=\mathscr{A}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{N}
. Define
\mu\colon\mathscr{E}_{T}\to\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
by
\mu(a\otimes f):=a\cdot(\nu+\omega\gamma)(f)
. Then
\mu(\mathscr{E}_{T})=\mathscr{F}^{*}
. And
G_{\bullet}\mu\colon\mathscr{E}_{T}\to B_{T}^{\dagger}
is given by
G_{\bullet}\mu(a\otimes f):=a\cdot\nu(f)
. But

\mathscr{A}_{T}\cdot\nu(\mathscr{N}_{T})=\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}
. Thus
G_{-p}\mu
restricts to
a surjection
(\mathscr{E}_{T})_{-p}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*})_{-p}
for all
p
; also

\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}\subset G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*}
, but equality cannot yet be asserted as
it’s not obvious
\mu
is strict.

For all
p
, note
\mathscr{E}_{-p}=\bigoplus_{q+r=p}\mathscr{A}_{-q}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{N}_{-r}
. Thus (
8.1.1
) gives
\mathop{\rm rank}\mathscr{E}_{-p}=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)
.

For
p\geq b_{1}
, note
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)
by
(
8.5
)(1) as
\mathbf{t}
is permissible. But
\mathscr{C}^{*}\in\mathbf{H}\Delta_{\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
where
\mathbf{h}:=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. So
\mathbf{h}^{*}(-p)=\mathbf{g}_{\bar{s}}(p)
. Thus
(\mathscr{E}_{T})_{-p}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*})_{-p}
is an isomorphism.

Form the induced map
\bar{\mu}\colon\mathscr{E}_{T}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}_{T}\to\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
. Then
G_{\bullet}\bar{\mu}
is injective, because it
factors as
\bigoplus_{p\geq b}(\mathscr{E}_{T})_{-p}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\bigoplus_{p\geq b}(C^{*}_{T})_{-p}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
. But owing to (
2.6
), the
functor
\mathscr{M}\mapsto G_{\bullet}\mathscr{M}
is biexact on the filtered
\mathscr{O}_{T}
-Artinian modules
\mathscr{M}
. Thus
\overline{\mu}
is a strict injection.

Note
\bar{\mu}
factors as
\mathscr{E}_{T}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}_{T}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}
since
\mathscr{F}^{*}
has the induced filtration. So
\bar{\mu}
gives a strict
isomorphism
\mathscr{E}_{T}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}_{T}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
. But
\overline{\mu}
is a strict injection. So
G_{\bullet}\bar{\mu}
gives
an isomorphism
(\mathscr{E}_{T})_{-p}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}G_{-p}\mathscr{F}^{*}
for
p\geq b_{1}
.
Thus
(\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*})_{-p}=G_{-p}\mathscr{F}^{*}\subset(\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})_{-p}
for

p\geq b_{1}
.

For
p<b_{1}
, note
\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
by
(
8.5
)(2). But
\mathscr{C}^{*}\in\mathbf{H}\Delta_{\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
where
\mathbf{h}:=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. So
\mathbf{h}^{*}(-p)=\mathbf{b}(p)
. But
(\mathscr{C}^{*}_{T})_{-p}\subset G_{-p}\mathscr{F}^{*}\subset(\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})_{-p}
. Further,
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{-p}\big/\mathscr{C}^{*}_{-p}
is flat,
because
\mathscr{C}^{*}\in\mathbf{H}\Delta_{\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}}^{\mathbf{h}^{*}}
. Thus
(\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*})_{-p}=G_{-p}\mathscr{F}^{*}=(\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger})_{-p}
. Thus
\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}=G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{*}
.

Finally, form the following commutative diagram, which is induced by

\mu
:

\kern 39.65619pt\kern-39.65619pt(F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}_{T})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}\mathscr{O}_{T}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.83313pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\mathscr{E}_{T}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}\mathscr{O}_{T}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.83313pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\bigl(\mathscr{E}_{T}\big/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}_{T}\bigr)\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}\mathscr{O}_{T}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.83313pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}0

\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$}}\Big\downarrow\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle$\hss}}

\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$}}\Big\downarrow\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle$\hss}}

\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle$}}\Big\downarrow\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle$\hss}}

\kern 39.65619pt\kern-38.66609pt(F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}\mathscr{O}_{T}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to17.81332pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\mathscr{F}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}\mathscr{O}_{T}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to18.80342pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}\bigl(\mathscr{F}^{*}\big/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}\bigr)\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}\mathscr{O}_{T}\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to17.81332pt{\kern 3.75pt\rightarrowfill\kern 3.75pt}}\limits$}0

(10.8.1)

Its rows are right exact. Its vertical maps are surjective; in fact,
the right map is an isomorphism, as the isomorphism
\mathscr{E}_{T}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}_{T}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
was proved above.

Note
\mathscr{E}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S}=(\mathscr{A}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{N})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathscr{O}_{S}=\mathscr{N}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{S}=\mathscr{N}
. But
F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{N}=0
as

\mathscr{C}^{*}
has local generator type
\mathbf{t}^{*}
and as
\mathbf{t}
is
permissible. In (
10.8.1
), the upper left map preserves the
filtrations. So it’s 0. So the lower left map is 0 too, as the left
vertical map is surjective. Thus (
10.8.1
) yields
\mathscr{N}_{T}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathscr{F}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}\mathscr{O}_{T}
. But this isomorphism is strict, as
\mathscr{E}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathscr{F}^{*}/F^{1-b_{1}}\mathscr{F}^{*}
is strict. Thus

\mathscr{N}_{T}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}G_{\bullet}(\mathscr{F}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}\mathscr{O}_{T})
. Thus
\mathscr{F}
has
the same socle type
\mathbf{t}
as
\mathscr{C}
. Thus
\mathscr{C}
is very effective.
∎

(10.9) Proposition.

Fix
\mathbf{h}
. Set
r_{p}:=\sum_{q<p}(\mathbf{b}(q)-\mathbf{h}(q))
and
\mathbf{R}:=\sum_{p}\mathbf{t}(p)r_{p}
. Given an effective
\mathscr{C}\in\mathbf{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{T}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
for some
T/S
, form the associated map
T\to\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. Then
T\times_{\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}}\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
is an affine-space bundle over
T
of fiber
dimension
\mathbf{R}
.

Proof.

Set
\mathbb{F}:=T\times_{\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}}\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. We must prove each point
x\in T
has a neighborhood

U_{x}
equipped with an isomorphism
\varphi_{x}\colon\mathbb{F}_{U_{x}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathbb{A}^{\mathbf{R}}_{U_{x}}
such that, on each overlap
U_{xy}:=U_{x}\cap U_{y}
, the map

(\varphi_{y}|U_{xy})(\varphi_{x}|U_{xy})^{-1}
is an affine transformation of

\mathbb{A}^{\mathbf{R}}_{U_{xy}}
. Notice that the cocycle condition automatically
holds.

Set
\mathscr{N}:=\mathscr{C}^{*}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{T}}\mathscr{O}_{T}
and set
\mathscr{W}:=\mathscr{B}_{T}^{\dagger}/\mathscr{C}_{T}^{*}
. Fix
p\leq 0
. For any
R/T
, set
\mathbf{V}_{p}(R):=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{R}}\bigl((\mathscr{N}_{p})_{R},\ (F^{p+1}\mathscr{W})_{R}\bigr)
. As
R

varies, the
\mathbf{V}_{p}(R)
form a functor; by
[
EGAI
]
*(9.6.1),
p. 377, it’s representable, say by
\mathbb{V}_{p}/T
. Also, note
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathscr{N}_{p})=\mathbf{t}(p)
and
\mathop{\rm rank}(F^{p+1}\mathscr{W})=r_{p}
. Thus
\dim(\mathbb{V}_{p}/T)=\mathbf{t}(p)r_{p}
.

Set
\mathbf{V}(R):=F^{1}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{R}}(\mathscr{N}_{R},\,\mathscr{W}_{R})
. Note
\mathbf{V}(R)=\bigoplus_{p}\mathbf{V}_{p}(R)
. Thus, as
R
varies, the
\mathbf{V}(R)
form a
functor, and it’s representable by the fiber product, say
\mathbb{V}/T
,
of the
\mathbb{V}_{p}/T
. Furthermore,
\dim(\mathbb{V}/T)=\mathbf{R}
.

Given
x\in T
, choose a neighborhood
U_{x}
such that
\mathscr{N}_{U_{x}}
and

\mathscr{W}_{U_{x}}
are free over
\mathscr{O}_{U_{x}}
. Choosing bases yields an
isomorphism
\psi_{x}\colon\mathbb{V}_{U_{x}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathbb{A}^{\mathbf{R}}_{U_{x}}
.

Also, then there exist homogeneous sections
\nu\colon\mathscr{N}_{U_{x}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{U_{x}}^{*}
of
\mathscr{C}_{U_{x}}^{*}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{N}_{U_{x}}
and
\omega\colon\mathscr{W}_{U_{x}}\hookrightarrow F^{-s}\mathscr{B}_{U_{x}}^{\dagger}
of
F^{-s}\mathscr{B}_{U_{x}}^{\dagger}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{W}_{R}
. But
\mathscr{C}
is effective for
T
. So for any affine
R/U_{x}
,
there exists a map of sets

F^{1}\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\mathscr{O}_{R}}(\mathscr{N}_{R},\,\mathscr{W}_{R})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\big\{\,\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{B_{R}}^{\mathbf{h}}\ \big|\ G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{C}_{R}\,\big\}

\gamma

\displaystyle\longmapsto\hphantom{\big\{\,}\mathscr{F}^{*}:=\mathscr{A}_{R}\cdot(\nu_{R}+\omega_{R}\gamma)(\mathscr{N}_{R}).

Owing to (
10.6
), this map is bijective. Plainly, it’s
functorial in
R
. So it defines an isomorphism
\theta_{x}\colon\mathbb{F}_{U_{x}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathbb{V}_{U_{x}}
. Set
\varphi_{x}:=\psi_{x}\theta_{x}
, so that
\varphi_{x}\colon\mathbb{F}_{U_{x}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\longrightarrow$}}\limits^{\sim}}\mathbb{A}^{\mathbf{R}}_{U_{x}}
.

The isomorphisms
\psi_{x}
and
\theta_{x}
depend on the choices of
several
\mathscr{O}_{U_{x}}
-maps, but two different choices of the same map
differ by an appropriate
\mathscr{O}_{U_{x}}
-isomorphism. Thus although

\varphi_{y}|U_{xy}
and
\varphi_{x}|U_{xy}
needn’t be the same isomorphism
from
\mathbb{F}_{U_{xy}}
to
\mathbb{A}^{\mathbf{R}}_{U_{xy}}
, nevertheless
(\varphi_{y}|U_{xy})(\varphi_{x}|U_{xy})^{-1}
is an affine transformation of
\mathbb{A}^{\mathbf{R}}_{U_{xy}}
.
∎

(10.10) Theorem.

Set
r_{p}:=\sum_{q<p}(\mathbf{b}(q)-\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(q))
and
\mathbf{R}:=\sum_{p}\mathbf{t}(p)r_{p}
. Assume that
\mathscr{A}
has integrity
s-b_{1}
,
and
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}
is
(1-b_{1})
-stable — for example, that
\mathscr{A}
is
the symmetric algebra on a locally free
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module and that
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}
. Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible and
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}\neq\emptyset
. Then
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
is an affine-space bundle over
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
of fiber dimension
\mathbf{R}
, and
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is one over
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
.

Proof.

Set
\mathbf{h}:=\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}
. Set
T:=\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
and
\mathbb{F}:=T\times_{\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}}\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
. Let
\mathscr{C}
be the universal quotient of
\mathscr{B}_{T}
. Then
\mathscr{C}
is (very) effective by (
10.8
). So
\mathbb{F}
is an affine-space bundle over
T
of fiber dimension
r
by
(
10.9
). Thus we must prove
\mathbb{F}=\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
.

Fix
R/S
. An
R
-point of
\mathbb{F}
is given by an
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}^{\mathbf{h}}
with
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{H}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
. So
\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
by (
10.5
) or (
10.8
). Thus
\mathscr{F}
gives an

R
-point of
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
. Conversely, an

R
-point of
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
is given by an
\mathscr{F}\in F\Psi_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
. Then
G_{\bullet}\mathscr{F}\in\mathbf{H}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
by (
10.4
)(1), (2) and
(
10.2
). Thus
\mathbb{F}
and
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h},\mathbf{t}}
have
the same
R
-points, so they’re equal as schemes.

The last assertion follows because
F\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}=\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}\times_{\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}}\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}}
essentially by
defintion; see (
4.13
).
∎

(10.11) Corollary.

Assume
\mathbf{t}
is permissible, and
S
is irreducible.

(1)
Assume
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
is nonempty.
Then it is irreducible.

(2)
Assume
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is nonempty. Then
it is open and dense in
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
.

Proof.

For (1),
recall that
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
is irreducible by (
9.8
)(1). So
\mathbb{F}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
is irreducible, as it’s an
affine space bundle over
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
by (
10.10
). Thus (1) holds

The proof of (2) is similar with (
9.8
)(2) in place of
(
9.8
)(1).
∎

(10.12) Corollary.

Set
\mathbf{F}:=\sum_{p}\mathbf{t}(p)\big(\sum_{q\leq p}(\mathbf{b}(q)-\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}(q))\bigr)
. Assume that
\mathscr{A}
has integrity
s-b_{1}
and
that
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}
is
(1-b_{1})
-stable — for example, that
\mathscr{A}
is
the symmetric algebra on a locally free
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module and that
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{A}
. Assume that
\mathbf{t}
is permissible.

(1)
Then
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is covered by
(possibly empty) open subschemes, each one isomorphic to an open
subscheme of the affine space over
S
of fiber dimension
\mathbf{F}
.

(2)
If
S
is irreducible, then so are
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
and
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
, but
they may be empty.

Proof.

First, note that
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is
nonempty iff
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is so.
Moreover, both (1) and (2) are trivial if these schemes are empty. So
assume they’re nonempty.

For (1),
note
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
and

\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
are both subschemes
of
\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}}}
by (
7.2
) and
(
6.7
). In fact,
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}\subset\mathbb{H}\Psi_{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{\bar{s}},\mathbf{t}}
, since the
universal quotient on
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is of

S
-socle type
t
by (
8.4
)(6). So
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is an affine-space bundle over
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
of fiber dimension
\mathbf{R}
by
(
10.10
). Thus (1) (
9.3
)(2) yields (1).

For (2),
note
\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}
is recursively maximal by
(
8.8
). Assume
S
is irreducible. Then
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is irreducible by (
7.8
) applied with

S_{\rm red}
for
S
. Hence
\mathbb{F}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
is irreducible, as it’s an affine-space bundle over
\mathbb{H}\Lambda_{\mathscr{B}}^{\{\mathbf{h}^{\rm I}_{m}\}}
by (
10.10
). Thus (2)
holds.

Alternatively, (2) follows immediately from (
9.8
) and
(
10.11
).
∎

(10.13) Example (smoothable quotients, elementary components).

Fix a polynomial ring
A:=k[X_{1},\dotsc,X_{r}]
with
k
a field. Let’s
see how (
10.12
) can help in finding examples of
k
-Artinian
quotients
C
of
A
that aren’t
smoothable
(deformable to

k
-etale quotients) and examples of
elementary
components of

\mathop{\rm Hilb}_{\mathbb{A}_{k}^{r}/k}^{d}
where
d:=\dim_{k}C
(absolutely irreducible components whose
K
-points for every
field
K\supset k
are quotients
C^{\prime}
of
A_{K}
with
\mathop{\rm Spec}(C^{\prime})
